MEMORANDUM 
To: 
Worcester County Commissioners 
From: Ed Welch, Procurement Officer 
Date: June 24, 2026 
Re: 
Request to Award – Solid Waste Environmental Monitoring Services 
Public Works-Solid Waste Division is recommending the County award the contract for  
Solid Waste Environmental Monitoring Services to EA Engineering, Science, and Technology, Inc.  
(Ocean Pines, MD).   The total funds requested are $373,030 which includes an additional $40,000 for Title V 
Tier 2 NMOC testing. Due to our historic knowledge of the County's sites and programs, EA's price includes the 
required Title V Tier 2 NMOC testing and reporting under task 1.2a, which totals $40,000. This occurs once 
every 5 years and would not be included in pricing in the additional 2 years if our contract was awarded 
and renewed. 
Proposals were due and opened on June 16, 2026, at 2 p.m. Four (4) proposals were received. I have attached 
the proposal tabulation and contract to this memo.  An evaluation team consisting of three members 
reviewed each proposal individually prior to an overall group average being established.   
General Ledger accounts for the funding sources are as follows: 
680.7002.6530.040 - $239,710 ($62,222 for gas monitoring, $177,488 for groundwater monitoring). $350,000 
was budgeted in this account for FY27 for gas and groundwater monitoring at cell 5. 
100.1203.200.6530.065 - $23,738. $40,000 was budgeted in this account for FY27 for MDE required gas 
monitoring at closed sites.  
100.1203.200.6530.070 - $109,582. $130,000 was budgeted in this account for FY27 for MDE required 
groundwater monitoring at closed sites. 
Please feel free to contact me if you have any questions. Thank you. 
Solid Waste Environmental Monitoring Services
Request for Proposals Tabulation Sheet
Tuesday, June 16, 2026, at 2:00 pm
Respondent's Name(s):
Soil & Land Use Technology (Washington, DC)
BAI Technology LLC (Annapolis, MD)
Geosyntec Consultants Inc. (Columbia, MD)
EA Engineering (Ocean Pines, MD)
ITEM 15
15 - 1

<!-- page 160 -->
12 
SECTION IV: SCOPE OF SERVICES 
A. PROJECT DESCRIPTION
1.
The County is accepting sealed proposals from qualified Consultants to provide
comprehensive environmental monitoring services at its four (4) solid waste disposal
facilities. The scope of work includes groundwater and surface water monitoring and air
quality (landfill gas) monitoring programs conducted in accordance with applicable Maryland
Department of the Environment (MDE) directives and regulatory requirements and in
accordance with the terms and conditions and specifications set forth in this solicitation.
B. CONTRACT PERIOD AND PRICING
1.
Project timeline is 365 days from Notice to Proceed.
2.
Pricing must include all labor, travel, materials, tools, and equipment to perform Work.
3.
Contract pricing will be valid for the term of 12 months (“Contract Period”).
4.
Pricing will not change during the Contract Period.
C. CONTRACT PERIOD RENEWAL OPTION
1.
The County reserves the right to extend the Contract Period for two additional one-year
terms, provided the Successful Vendor agrees there will be no increase in price for the
additional Contract Period(s).  The Successful Vendor will notify the County’s
Administration Office in writing by each subsequent year regarding the Successful Vendor’s
decision to hold prices firm for an additional year.  The County will then have the option to
either extend the Contract Period or rebid.
D. SCOPE OF WORK
1.
Central Site Landfill
The Central Site Landfill is subject to Title V (40 CFR Part 70) requirements, the Greenhouse
Gas Mandatory Reporting Program (40 CFR Part 98), and municipal solid waste landfill
groundwater monitoring requirements under COMAR 26.04.07 in conjunction with 40 CFR
Part 258. The following tasks describe the monitoring, reporting, and certification activities
required to demonstrate compliance with applicable state and federal regulations.  Ground
water, Surface Water and Leachate monitoring locations are provided in Attachment A.
i.
Task 1: Central Site Landfill Air Monitoring. Provide routine monitoring
of landfill gas and air emissions at the central landfill facility. Monitoring
shall include all perimeter landfill gas probes, on-site structures, and any
additional compliance monitoring locations. Submit following reports and
certifications to MDE/ARMA in accordance with permit (Appendix B1)
deadlines.
a.
Title V Requirements
i.
Prepare and submit Semi-Annual Monitoring Report (EPA
Form SIXMON)  in accordance with MDE/ARMA
requirements.
ii.
Prepare and submit Annual Emissions Certification Report
and Air Toxics Certification in compliance with
MDE/ARMA requirements, including the Annual Non-
Methane Organic Compound (NMOC) Emission Rate
Report.
iii.
Prepare and submit Annual Title V Compliance Certification
(EPA Form A-COMP) Report summarizing the compliance
status of each permit condition for the prior calendar year
(January 1 through December 31).
ITEM 15
15 - 3

<!-- page 161 -->
13 
b.
Greenhouse Gas (GHG) Mandatory Reporting
i.
Perform greenhouse gas emissions calculations in
accordance with 40 CFR Part 98 subpart HH (municipal
solid waste landfills) and submit using the e-GGRT
application.
ii.
Task 2: Central Site Landfill Groundwater, Surface Water and
Leachate Monitoring.  Provide routine monitoring of groundwater, surface
water, and leachate at the landfill facility in accordance with the refuse
disposal permit (Attachment B2) and the approved Monitoring Plan
(Attachment C). Monitoring shall include all compliance monitoring wells,
surface water sampling locations, leachate collection points, and any
additional compliance monitoring locations.
a.
Regular Sampling and Analysis. Perform sampling using low-flow
sampling techniques for all monitoring wells throughout the site.
i.
Semi-Annual Sampling Events (Spring) will include:
1.
22 shallow monitoring wells
2.
3 intermediate monitoring wells
3.
2 potable supply wells
4.
2 surface water sampling locations
5.
5 leachate collection points
ii.
Annual Sampling Event (Fall) will include:
1.
22 shallow monitoring wells
2.
8 intermediate monitoring wells
3.
3 deep monitoring wells
4.
2 potable supply wells
5.
2 surface water locations
6.
5 leachate collection points
iii.
Analytical parameters will include all constituents required
under Tables I and II of the refuse disposal permit. Leachate
samples will additionally be analyzed for semi-volatile
organic compounds, pesticides, polychlorinated biphenyls
(PCBs), cyanide, and sulfide. Method 8011 will be used for
the analysis of 1,2-dibromoethane (EDB) and 1,2-dibromo-
3-chloropropane (DBCP) per MDE directive.
iv.
Quality Control (QC) samples required per the approved
Monitoring Plan.
v.
Perform static water level gauging at each monitoring in
accordance with the approved Monitoring Plan.
b.
PFAS Sampling and Analysis.
i.
PFAS sampling is required at the landfill during both the
Fall and Spring semi-annual events, for a total of two PFAS
sampling events.
ii.
PFAS sampling will include the full monitoring well
network and will incorporate any additional sampling
protocols required for PFAS Method 1633 analysis.
ITEM 15
15 - 4

<!-- page 162 -->
14 
c.
Groundwater Contour Mapping, Statistical Analysis, and
Reporting.
i.
Following each sampling event, prepare a semi-annual report
documenting water quality findings, statistical analysis, and
groundwater conditions at the landfill in accordance with
permit requirements and approved Monitoring Plan.
ii.
One electronic copy of each report will be submitted to
MDE on behalf of the County. Two hard copies will also be
provided to the County. Reports will be submitted to MDE
in accordance with permit requirements.
iii.
An annual coordination meeting will be conducted with
County staff to review monitoring results, discuss regulatory
status, and plan for upcoming sampling events.
2.
Closed Landfills (Berlin, Pocomoke, and Snow Hill).
The following tasks describe the monitoring and reporting activities required to demonstrate
compliance with applicable state and federal regulations for the closed Berlin, Pocomoke and
Snow Hill landfills.  Monitoring locations are provided in Attachment D.
a.
Task 3: Closed Landfill Air Monitoring. Provide landfill gas monitoring at
closed landfill facilities in accordance with COMAR 26.04.07, 40 CFR Part 258
Subpart D, and approved Environmental Monitoring Plan (Attachments E1, E2 and
E3). Submit reports MDE in accordance with permit deadlines. One electronic
copy of each report will be submitted to MDE on behalf of the County. Two hard
copies will also be provided to the County.
i.
Sampling Events include:
a.
7 permanent landfill gas probes and 1 on-site building at Pocomoke
(quarterly monitoring)
b.
21 permanent landfill gas probes and 1 on-site structure at Snow Hill
(bi-monthly monitoring)
c.
14 permanent landfill gas probes and 2 on-site structures at Berlin
(monthly)
b.
Task 4: Closed Landfill Groundwater Monitoring. Provide routine monitoring
of groundwater at the closed landfill facilities in accordance with approved
Environmental Monitoring Plan. Submit reports MDE in accordance with permit
deadlines. One electronic copy of each report will be submitted to MDE on behalf
of the County. Two hard copies will also be provided to the County.
i.
Semi-Annual Sampling (Spring and Fall) Events include:
a.
4 monitoring wells at Pocomoke
b.
6 monitoring wells at Snow Hill
c.
8 monitoring wells at Berlin
d.
3 shallows wells at Berlin for water level gauging
3.
Laboratory Requirements
a.
All groundwater analysis will be performed by an MDE-certified independent
laboratory.
b.
A signed statement from the proposed laboratory stating that they can meet the
Practical Quantitation Limit (PQL) concentration for each required parameter must
ITEM 15
15 - 5

<!-- page 163 -->
15 
be provided.  If the laboratory cannot meet the PQL and they do not have an 
approved waiver by MDE then they will not be approved for this contract. 
c.
Use of the most sensitive analytical methods listed with EPA guidance and in
federal regulations 40 CFR 136, 141, 143, and SW-846 for water quality analysis
4.
Additional Reporting Requirements
a.
Historical data is to be managed in an EQuIS database or approved equal (not excel
alone). All data, including non-detects should include numerical values such as
reporting limits (i.e., the use of ND is not adequate).
b.
Statistical analysis should be completed in accordance with 40 CFR 258.53,
“ground-water sampling and analysis requirements”. Use nonparametric test that
does not assume a data distribution. To minimize false positive error, perform data
testing so Type I error rate is below the minimum allowable under 40 CFR
258.52(h)(2). Gehan procedure treats non-detects as less than values as opposed to
using a simple substitution such as one-half the detection limit. Sample
concentrations below the PQL were treated as <PQL in the ranking procedure. The
calculated U for each well/analyte pair is compared to a critical value (Ucrit) at the
99 percent confidence level. The upgradient (background) condition should be
compared to each downgradient well separately. A statistical table should be
prepared to summarize the statistical results, showing the number of observations
and hits for each constituent at upgradient and downgradient wells, and average
and maximum detected values for each constituent.
c.
Tables including groundwater analytical results (groundwater wells, trip blanks,
field blanks, and rinsate blanks), relative percent differences, PFAS hazard
summary, average indicator parameter concentration in the upgradient wells and
the downgradient wells, and statistical table for analytes exceeding upgradient well
concentrations at the 99th percent significance level are required.
d.
Graphs should include all historical MCL exceedances that have occurred at the
site.
e.
Groundwater contour map should be developed using most recent groundwater
elevation data and aerial topography.

SECTION V: APPROACH, EXPERIENCE, STAFFING AND 
QUALIFICATIONS 
A. TECHNICAL APPROACH 
1. The Vendor shall submit a technical approach of up to six (6) pages describing their approach 
to performing the services required under this contract. The technical approach shall 
demonstrate the Vendor's understanding of the project objectives, the technical and logistical 
challenges involved, and the methods by which the Vendor intends to meet the County's 
scope, quality, and schedule requirements.  
2. At a minimum, the technical approach shall address the following: 
a. Approach and Methodology. The Vendor shall describe its proposed methodology 
for each major task or phase of work, including the methods, tools, standards, and 
analytical frameworks to be applied. The description shall be sufficiently detailed to 
demonstrate a clear and informed plan for executing the work, rather than a general 
understanding of the requirements. Where multiple approaches are available, the 
Vendor shall identify the proposed approach and explain the basis for its selection. 
b. Scope of Services. The Vendor shall demonstrate its understanding of the full scope 
of work and describe how the proposed services address each element. Any proposed 
modifications, clarifications, or additions to the scope described in this solicitation 
shall be clearly identified and explained. The Vendor shall also confirm that the 
scope can be performed in its entirety using its own staff and any proposed 
subconsultants. 
c. Project Schedule and Milestones. The Vendor shall provide a project schedule 
identifying major tasks, milestones, and deliverable submission dates. The Vendor 
shall identify potential schedule risks or dependencies, including those contingent on 
County actions, and describe how such risks will be managed. 
d. Quality Assurance and Quality Control. The Vendor shall describe their QA/QC 
procedures for all major deliverables, including how work products will be internally 
reviewed prior to submission, who is responsible for QA/QC oversight, and what 
standards or checklists will be applied. The QA/QC description shall be specific to 
this project and shall not consist solely of a general firm policy statement. 
e. Anticipated Challenges and Risk Management. The Vendor shall identify any 
aspects of the project that present technical, logistical, or schedule risk, and describe 
their approach to managing or mitigating those risks. This section should reflect a 
realistic and project-specific assessment, not a generic list of potential issues. 
f. Progress Reports. The Vendor must submit a progress report with each invoice 
detailing the work performed that is being billed, outstanding issues, summary of 
additional work requests, percent completed, any anticipated delays, etc. 
B. STAFFING 
1. Roles, Responsibilities, and Coordination. The Vendor shall describe how responsibilities 
will be allocated among team members and subconsultants, how the team will be organized 
and managed, and how internal coordination will be maintained throughout the project. The 
technical approach shall identify the proposed Project Manager and describe their role in day-
to-day project oversight, client communication, and schedule management. 
2. Staffing and Qualifications. The Vendor shall provide detailed information regarding the 
qualifications, relevant experience, and anticipated availability of key personnel proposed to 
perform the work under this Contract. The individuals identified as key staff shall be 
considered a material representation of the team assigned to the project, and the County’s 
award decision will be based, in part, on the qualifications of those individuals. 
ITEM 15
15 - 7

<!-- page 165 -->
17 
 
a. At a minimum, the following key staff are required: 
i. 
Project Manager: PE licensed in Maryland with 5 years of project 
management experience in solid waste disposal facility design, permitting 
and reporting. 
ii. Project Engineer/Scientist: Environmental professional, with a Bachelor’s 
degree in civil/environmental engineering, environmental sciences, or 
related field with 5 years of experience in solid waste disposal facility 
design, permitting, and reporting. 
3. Subconsultant Integration. If subconsultants are proposed, the work plan shall describe the 
specific scope of services each subconsultant will perform, how their work will be integrated 
with that of the prime, and how the prime will ensure quality and schedule compliance across 
the full team. 
C. ORGANIZATIONAL CAPACITY AND CURRENT WORKLOAD 
1. Proposers shall submit a statement describing their firm's current capacity to perform the work 
required under this contract. The statement shall address, at a minimum, the following: 
a. A summary of current active projects, including project name, client, estimated 
construction value or contract value, current phase, and anticipated completion date for 
each 
b. The current billable utilization rate or workload level of key personnel proposed for 
assignment to this project, expressed as a percentage of available time or in hours per 
week. 
c. Identification of any known conflicts, scheduling constraints, or competing deadlines 
that could affect the availability of proposed staff during the anticipated performance 
period. 
d. A description of specialized equipment, software, or technical resources owned or 
leased by the firm that would be applied to this project, including hydraulic modeling 
platforms, field investigation equipment, GIS capabilities, or other relevant tools. 
e. If subconsultants are proposed, the same workload and capacity information shall be 
provided for each subconsultant firm. 
2. Organizational Chart. For every individual proposed for this contract and shown on the 
organization chart, the chart shall clearly show their company affiliation and their permanent 
office location.  The Organization Chart shall be limited to an 11 x 17-inch page with Times New 
Roman font size of 10-point or larger. 
D. EXAMPLE PROJECTS 
1. The Vendor shall provide at least three and no more than five example projects similar in scope to 
the requested scope of work within the past 6 years. Each project should be limited to one page, 
including images.  The Vendor shall also identify key staff that worked on each project and the 
Owner’s contact information. 
END OF SECTION

Subject: Over Expenditure   
The Department of Emergency Services, Sheriff’s Office, Snow Hill Police Department, and 
Worcester County Jail depends on a software package known as “NeverFail” for the operation of 
the servers that support the Computer Aided Dispatch, Records Management, and Jail 
Management systems of Worcester County. An increase in cost as well as an overlap in fiscal 
year have been identified. This results in a required over-expenditure in FY26 in the amount of 
$53,273.81, G/L 100.1102.044.6130.070, to support continued operation of these critical 
systems. 
The Department respectfully requests approval of this over-expenditure to ensure continued 
support of this system.

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
Weston S. Young, P.E. Chief Administrative Officer 
Candace Savage, CGFM Deputy Chief Administrative Officer 
FROM: 
Ed Welch, Procurement Officer 
DATE: 
June 22, 2026 
RE: 
Over-expenditure – Fire Training Center – Logistical Storage Building Project 
Fire Marshal’s Office is requesting that the Commissioners review and approve an over-expenditure for the  
Fire Training Center – Logistical Storage Building Project in the amount of $135,898.65 for the five (5) change orders 
from Whayland Company LLC as shown below: 
•
Change order #1 - extension of the perimeter fencing providing security for the facility.  This fencing is proposed
to extend approximately an additional 100 feet to the east from its original proposed location.  We are also
proposing a 12’ double swing gate to provide access and egress from this area.  This proposed Change Order is in
the amount of $10,083.11.
•
Change order #2 - upsizing of the water service line from 4” to 6” (which is the county minimum for servicing a
fire hydrant).  This includes piping, fittings, and the installation of a fire hydrant.  This proposed Change Order is
in the amount of $136.77.
•
Change order #3 - addition of a yard water hydrant, a 2” water line, a sanitary cleanout, and a French drain.  This
proposed Change Order is in the amount of $15,411.27.
•
Change order #4 - proposed mill and overlay of the entire Fire Training Center parking lot.  The original project
only included the paving of disturb areas and the area surrounding the new training tower.  This change order
would provide the milling and overlay of the entire parking lot (there would be no patching, the entire area would
be paved at one time).  This parking lot has not been paved in over thirty years.  This proposed Change Order is in
the amount of $97,761.22.
•
Change order #5 - furnishing and installing an additional direct buried 2” PVC conduit to provide electricity to the
new training tower and the associated area.  This proposed Change Order is in the amount of $12,506.28.
Change orders #1 (fencing) and #4 (pavement) are for the Fire Training Center as a whole.  The fencing surrounds both 
the Storage Building and the Training Tower and the pavement will provide access to both.  Whayland advised if we leave 
the paving as it is in the contract, it will only include patch repairs.  #3 (water yard hydrant/French drain/etc.) has work 
which is also for both areas.  #2 (fire hydrant piping) and #5 (electrical work) are both for the training tower. However, 
due to planned re-paving it is best to do this work at the same time. 
The Commissioner approved a contract with the Whayland Company LLC in October 2025 for $3,361,500.00. The new 
total requested expenditure with Whayland including these change orders is $3,497,398.65. Request approval of 
$135,898.65  in available FY25 Assigned Funds from the Fire Tower Replacement to be used for this over-expenditure. 
Please feel free to contact me if you have any questions. Thank you. 
cc: Matt Owen 
      Robert Korb Jr.

TO: 
Worcester County Commissioners 
FROM: 
Kim Reynolds, Budget Officer 
DATE: 
June 23, 2026 
RE: 
FY26 Over-expenditure Request Private Pre-K 
Please see the attached invoice from the Maryland State Department of Education (MSDE) for Private Pre-K 
expenditures incurred in Worcester County during Fiscal Year 2026. 
Administration is requesting an over-expenditure totaling $406,662.90 in account 100.1505.200.7120.332. 
According to MSDE, this amount differs from the information provided in October 2025, which indicated a balance 
of $0.  
Specifically, the Private Pre-K budget was initially developed based on the number of slots awarded to private 
providers according to the providers’ geographic locations. However, counties are ultimately billed based on the 
actual students served and the students’ counties of residence. As a result, final county obligations differ from the 
preliminary estimates provided during the budget process.

RE: 
FY27 Over-expenditure request West OC EMS Budget 
Administration is requesting approval for an over-expenditure of $97,893 in the West OC EMS Supplement 
Account (General Ledger Account 100.1105.130.7080.020). A recently discovered spreadsheet formula error 
resulted in an understatement of the funding required for this account.

ensuring accurate funding calculations and improved transparency.

Worcester County

To:

From:  Brian Jones, IT Director
Re: 
Requesting IT Policy Revision Approval 
Date: 
June 22, 2026 
I am writing to request formal approval from the Commissioners of our updated IT Security Policy, which 
now includes comprehensive guidance on the use of Artificial Intelligence (AI) technologies within our 
organization. 
As AI tools and capabilities become increasingly integrated into daily operations, it is essential that we 
establish clear standards to ensure their safe, compliant, and responsible use. The updated policy 
incorporates: 
• Controls to mitigate AI-related cybersecurity risks
• Requirements for data protection when using AI systems
• Standards for evaluating and approving AI tools
• Guidelines for ethical and secure deployment within business workflows
These additions strengthen our security posture, reduce organizational risk, and align our practices with 
modern industry standards. 
The commissioner’s approval will allow us to move forward with using AI tools in the new FY. 
Please let me know if you have any questions or would like to review any section in more detail. Thank 
you for your time and consideration.

1. Purpose
The purpose of the Worcester County Government Acceptable Use Policy is to establish 
acceptable practices regarding the use of Worcester County Government Information 
Resources to protect the confidentiality, integrity, and availability of information created, 
collected, and maintained by Worcester County Government (WCG). 
2. Scope
This policy applies to: 
•
Any individual, entity, or process that interacts with any Worcester County Government
Information Resource
•
All employees, contractors, consultants, volunteers, and third-party personnel
•
All WCG-owned, leased, or managed systems, networks, devices, and data
Exclusions 
See section 8.2 of this policy 
3. Definitions
The following terms are used throughout this policy. For a complete list of definitions, see 
Appendix A. 
Term
Definition
Cloud Computing 
Applications
Software or services hosted and delivered over the internet rather 
than on local servers or personal computers.
Confidential Information
Data that must not be disclosed to unauthorized individuals due to 
legal, contractual, or business obligations, where unauthorized 
disclosure may result in financial or reputational harm to WCG.
Information Resources
All computerized communications hardware, software, and systems 
owned, leased, licensed to, or accessible through WCG, made 
available for official or limited personal use. Includes computers, 
phones, devices capable of transmitting or receiving data, voice 
communications, email, text messages, and other forms of 
electronic or digital communication.
ITEM 21
21 - 3

<!-- page 183 -->
Term
Definition
Internal Information
Information created or held by WCG for internal operational 
purposes that, if disclosed externally, could cause harm to citizens, 
partners, or WCG business interests.
Jail-broken / Rooted 
Device 
A mobile device on which the operating system's built-in security 
restrictions have been bypassed or removed, enabling unauthorized 
software or elevated system access.
Mobile Device
Any portable computing or communications device, including 
smartphones, tablets, and laptops, capable of connecting to WCG 
Information Resources.
Need to Know
The principle that access to information is granted only to 
individuals whose role requires that access to perform their duties.
Personally Owned Mobile 
Device
A mobile device owned by an employee or contractor rather than by 
WCG.
Remote Wipe
The ability to remotely erase all data from a mobile device in the 
event of loss, theft, or policy violation.
Removable Media
Portable data storage devices including USB drives, external hard 
drives, SD cards, CDs, DVDs, and similar devices that can be 
connected to or disconnected from WCG Information Resources.
Security Awareness 
Training
Mandatory training is designed to educate personnel on information 
security risks, responsibilities, and best practices.
4. Policy Statement
Worcester County Government Information Resources are provided to facilitate WCG business 
operations and must be used in a manner that protects the confidentiality, integrity, and 
availability of WCG information and systems. All personnel are responsible for complying with 
this policy when using WCG Information Resources and/or when conducting WCG business. 
Use of WCG Information Resources constitutes acknowledgement of an agreement to abide by 
this policy. 
All use of WCG Information Resources must comply with applicable local, state, and federal 
laws and regulations. 
5. Policy Requirements
ITEM 21
21 - 4

<!-- page 184 -->
5.1 Acceptable Use — General 
•
Personnel are responsible for complying with all WCG policies when using WCG
Information Resources. If requirements or responsibilities are unclear, seek assistance
from the Information Security Committee.
•
Personnel must promptly report harmful events or policy violations involving WCG
assets or information to their manager or a member of the IT Department, including
technology incidents, data incidents, unauthorized access incidents, facility security
incidents, and policy violations.
•
Personnel must not engage in activities that may harass, threaten, impersonate, or
abuse others; degrade the performance of WCG Information Resources; deprive
authorized personnel of access; obtain resources beyond those allocated; or circumvent
WCG computer security measures.
•
Personnel must not download, install, or run security programs or utilities that reveal or
exploit weaknesses in system security, including password cracking programs, packet
sniffers, or port scanners, on any WCG Information Resource without IT authorization.
•
All inventions, intellectual property, and proprietary information developed on WCG time
and/or using WCG Information Resources are the property of Worcester County
Government.
•
Use of encryption must be managed in a manner that allows authorized WCG personnel
to promptly access all data.
•
WCG Information Resources must not be used for personal financial gain.
•
Personnel must cooperate with incident investigations, including any federal or state
investigations.
•
Personnel must respect and comply with all legal protections provided by patents,
copyrights, trademarks, and intellectual property rights for any software or materials
viewed, used, or obtained using WCG Information Resources.
•
Personnel must not intentionally access, create, store, or transmit material deemed
offensive, indecent, or obscene.
5.2 Access Management 
•
Access to information is based on a need-to-know basis, authorized by an appropriate
Department Head or Information Technology staff.
•
Personnel may use only those network and host addresses issued to them by WCG IT
and must not attempt to access data or programs for which they do not have
authorization.
•
All remote access connections to WCG internal networks must be made through
approved, WCG-provided virtual private networks (VPNs).
•
Personnel must not divulge access information to anyone not specifically authorized to
receive it, including IT support personnel.
•
Personnel must not share personal authentication information including account
passwords, PINs, security tokens, multi-factor authentication information, access cards
and/or keys, or digital certificates.
•
Access cards and/or keys that are no longer required must be returned to physical
security personnel (HR or IT).
•
Lost or stolen access cards, security tokens, and/or keys must be reported to physical
security personnel, Department Head or Human Resources as soon as possible.
ITEM 21
21 - 5

<!-- page 185 -->
5.3 Authentication & Passwords 
• 
All personnel must maintain the confidentiality of personal authentication information. 
• 
Group or shared authentication information must be maintained solely among authorized 
members of that group. 
• 
Passwords must meet all WCG requirements including minimum length, complexity, and 
reuse history; must not be easily tied to the account owner; must not be the same as 
passwords used for non-business purposes; and should be unique for each system 
where possible. 
• 
User account passwords must not be divulged to anyone. WCG support personnel and 
contractors should never ask for user account passwords. 
• 
If the security of a password is in doubt, it must be changed immediately. 
• 
Personnel must not circumvent password entry using application remembering, 
embedded scripts, or hard-coded passwords in client software. 
• 
Security tokens must be returned on demand or upon termination of the relationship with 
WCG. 
 
5.4 Clear Desk / Clear Screen 
• 
Personnel must log off from applications or network services when they are no longer 
needed and/or at the end of a workday. 
• 
Personnel must log off or lock workstations and laptops when their workspace is 
unattended. 
• 
Physical and electronic keys used to access confidential information must not be left 
unattended in an unsecured workspace. 
• 
Laptops must be locked with a cable or secured in a locked drawer or cabinet when 
unattended, unless the laptop is encrypted. 
• 
Passwords must not be posted in any physically accessible location. 
• 
Copies of documents containing confidential information must be immediately removed 
from printers and fax machines. 
 
5.5 Data Security 
• 
Personnel must use approved encrypted communication methods when sending 
confidential information over public networks. 
• 
Only cloud computing applications authorized by a Department Head and/or IT 
Management may be used for sharing, storing, and transferring confidential or internal 
information. 
• 
Information must be appropriately shared, handled, transferred, saved, and destroyed 
based on its classification, as directed by a Department Head and/or IT staff. 
• 
Personnel must not discuss confidential information in public places or over insecure 
communication channels. 
• 
Confidential information must be transported by a WCG employee or a courier approved 
by County Administration. 
• 
All electronic media containing confidential information must be securely disposed of. 
Contact IT for guidance or assistance. 
ITEM 21
21 - 6

<!-- page 186 -->
5.6 Email & Electronic Communications 
• 
Auto-forwarding electronic messages outside of WCG internal systems are prohibited. 
• 
Electronic communications must not misrepresent the originator or WCG. 
• 
Personnel are responsible for all accounts assigned to them and for all actions taken 
with those accounts. 
• 
Accounts must not be shared without prior IT authorization, except for calendars and 
related calendaring functions. 
• 
Employees must not use personal email accounts to send or receive WCG confidential 
information. 
• 
Personal use of WCG-provided email must not involve solicitation; be associated with 
any political entity; harm WCG's reputation; forward chain emails; promote anti-social or 
unethical behavior; violate applicable laws; result in unauthorized disclosure of 
confidential information; include unauthorized signature content; or otherwise violate 
WCG policies. 
• 
Personnel must only send confidential information using approved secure electronic 
messaging solutions. 
• 
Personnel must use caution when responding to, clicking links within, or opening 
attachments in electronic communications. When uncertain, contact IT. 
• 
Personnel must use discretion in disclosing confidential or internal information in 
automated responses such as Out of Office messages. 
 
5.7 Hardware & Software 
• 
All hardware must be formally approved by IT Management before being connected to 
WCG networks. 
• 
Software installed on WCG equipment must be approved by IT Management and 
installed by WCG IT personnel. 
• 
All WCG assets taken off-site must be physically always secured. 
• 
Personnel traveling to a High-Risk location, as defined by the FBI and Office of Foreign 
Asset Control, must contact IT for approval before traveling with WCG assets. 
• 
Employees must not allow others, such as family members or other non-employees to 
access WCG Information Resources. 
• 
Unapproved hardware or software is subject to be removed from all WCG networks and 
Information Resources. 
 
5.8 Internet Use 
• 
The Internet must not be used to communicate WCG confidential or internal information 
unless the confidentiality and integrity of the information is ensured and the identity of 
recipients is established. 
• 
WCG networking and computing resources must only be used for business-related 
Internet activities. Prohibited activities include recreational games, personal social 
media, accessing or distributing pornographic or sexually oriented materials, and 
attempting unauthorized entry to any network or computer. 
ITEM 21
21 - 7

<!-- page 187 -->
• 
Access to the Internet from outside WCG facilities using WCG-owned hardware must 
adhere to the same policies that apply to use from within WCG facilities. 
 
5.9 Mobile Devices & BYOD 
• 
Personally owned mobile devices are not permitted to connect to the WCG corporate 
internal network. 
• 
Mobile devices that access WCG email must have a PIN or other authentication 
mechanism enabled. 
• 
Confidential information may only be stored on devices encrypted in compliance with the 
WCG Encryption Standard. 
• 
WCG confidential information must not be stored on any personally owned mobile 
device. 
• 
Theft or loss of any mobile device used to create, store, or access WCG confidential or 
internal information must be reported to WCG IT immediately. 
• 
All county-owned mobile devices must maintain up-to-date versions of all software and 
applications. 
• 
Jail-broken or rooted devices must not be used to connect to WCG Information 
Resources. 
• 
WCG IT Management may execute remote wipe capabilities for mobile devices without 
warning (see Mobile Device Email Acknowledgement). 
• 
In the event of a suspected incident or breach associated with a mobile device, the 
device may be removed from the personnel's possession as part of a formal 
investigation. 
• 
All mobile device usage in relation to WCG Information Resources may be monitored at 
the discretion of WCG IT Management. 
• 
WCG IT support for personally owned mobile devices is limited to assistance in 
complying with this policy and does not extend to device usability troubleshooting. 
• 
Texting or emailing while driving is not permitted while working for WCG or using WCG 
resources. Only hands-free calling while driving is permitted. 
 
5.10 Physical Security 
• 
Photographic, video, audio, or other recording equipment is not allowed in secure areas. 
• 
Personnel must always display photo ID access cards while in WCG buildings. 
• 
Personnel must badge into access-controlled areas. Piggybacking, tailgating, door 
propping, and any other activity to circumvent door access controls are prohibited. 
• 
Visitors accessing card-controlled areas must be always accompanied by authorized 
personnel. 
• 
Eating or drinking are not allowed in data centers. Caution must be used when eating or 
drinking near workstations or information processing facilities. 
 
5.11 Privacy 
• 
Information created, sent, received, or stored on WCG Information Resources is not 
private and may be accessed by WCG IT employees at any time, under the direction of 
ITEM 21
21 - 8

<!-- page 188 -->
WCG executive management and/or Human Resources, without knowledge of the user 
or resource owner. 
• 
WCG may log, review, and otherwise utilize any information stored on or passing 
through its Information Resource systems. 
• 
Personnel with extended system privileges must not access files or information not 
specifically required to carry out an employment-related task. 
 
5.12 Removable Media 
• 
Use of removable media for storage of WCG information must be supported by a 
reasonable business case and approved by WCG IT prior to use. 
• 
Personally owned removable media must not be used for storage of WCG information. 
• 
Personnel must not connect removable media from an unknown origin without prior IT 
approval. 
• 
Confidential and internal WCG information must not be stored on removable media 
without encryption. 
• 
All removable media must be stored in a safe and secure environment. 
• 
Loss or theft of any removable media that may have contained WCG information must 
be reported to WCG IT immediately. 
 
5.13 Security Training & Awareness 
• 
All new personnel must complete an approved security awareness training course prior 
to, or within 30 days of, being granted access to any WCG Information Resource. 
• 
All personnel must acknowledge receipt of and agreement to adhere to WCG 
Information Security policies before being granted access to WCG Information 
Resources. 
• 
All personnel must complete the annual security awareness training. 
 
5.14 Social Media 
• 
All social media communications must comply with applicable WCG policies. 
• 
Personnel are personally responsible for the content they publish online. 
• 
Creating any public social media account intended to represent WCG — including 
accounts that could reasonably be assumed to be official — requires permission from 
the WCG Communications Department. 
• 
When discussing WCG or WCG-related matters online, personnel must identify 
themselves by name and as a WCG representative and must make clear they are 
speaking for themselves unless explicitly authorized to speak on behalf of WCG. 
• 
When publishing WCG-relevant content online in a personal capacity, a disclaimer must 
accompany the content (e.g., "The opinions and content are my own and do not 
necessarily represent Worcester County Government's position or opinion."). 
• 
Online content must not violate applicable laws including copyright, fair use, financial 
disclosure, or privacy laws. 
• 
Content affiliated with WCG that discriminates based on age, sex, race, color, creed, 
religion, ethnicity, sexual orientation, gender identity or expression, national origin, 
ITEM 21
21 - 9

<!-- page 189 -->
citizenship, disability, marital status, or any other legally protected basis will not be 
tolerated. 
• 
Confidential information, internal communications, and non-public financial or 
operational information must not be published online in any form. 
• 
Personal information belonging to residents or customers must not be published online. 
• 
Personnel approved to post, review, or approve content on WCG social media sites 
must follow the WCG Social Media Management Procedures. 
 
5.15 Voicemail 
• 
Personnel must use discretion in disclosing confidential or internal information in 
voicemail greetings, including employment data, internal telephone numbers, location 
information, or other sensitive data. 
• 
Personnel must not access another user's voicemail account unless explicitly authorized. 
• 
Confidential information must not be disclosed in voicemail messages. 
 
5.16 Incidental Use 
• 
As a convenience, incidental personal use of WCG Information Resources is permitted 
for approved personnel only, subject to the following restrictions: 
◦ 
Incidental personal use does not extend to family members or other acquaintances 
◦ 
Incidental use must not result in direct costs to WCG 
◦ 
Incidental use must not interfere with normal job performance 
◦ 
No files or documents may be sent or received that may cause legal action against 
or embarrassment to WCG 
◦ 
Storage of personal email messages, voice messages, files, and documents on 
WCG Information Resources must be nominal 
◦ 
All information located on WCG Information Resources is owned by WCG, may be 
subject to open records requests, and may be accessed in accordance with this 
policy 
 
6. Roles & Responsibilities 
 
The following roles are responsible for implementing and maintaining this policy: 
 
Role / Title
Responsibilities
All Personnel
• 
Comply with this policy and all referenced WCG policies 
when using WCG Information Resources 
• 
Promptly report harmful events, security incidents, or policy 
violations to their manager or the IT Department 
• 
Complete mandatory security awareness training within 
required timeframes 
ITEM 21
21 - 10

<!-- page 190 -->

•

•

•

7. Implementation & Procedures 
 
7.1 Policy Acknowledgement 
All personnel must acknowledge receipt of an agreement to abide by this policy before being 
granted access to WCG Information Resources. Acknowledgement shall be obtained by Human 
Resources during the onboarding process and re-obtained whenever a major revision to this 
policy is issued. Records of acknowledgement shall be retained by Human Resources. 
 
7.2 Security Awareness Training 
The IT Department, in coordination with Human Resources, is responsible for administering 
mandatory security awareness training. Training shall be assigned to all personnel and must be 
completed: 
• 
Within 30 days of hire or contract commencement for new personnel 
• 
Annually for all existing personnel 
• 
Following any material revision to this policy, as determined by the Information Security 
Committee 
 
Training completion shall be tracked and reported to the Information Security Committee. 
Personnel who fail to complete required training within the prescribed timeframe may have 
access to WCG Information Resources suspended until training is completed. 
 
7.3 Access Provisioning & Revocation 
Access to WCG Information Resources shall be provided on a need-to-know basis following 
written authorization from the relevant Department Head. The IT Department shall revoke 
access promptly upon notification of termination, resignation, contract expiration, or role change. 
Human Resources and Department Heads are responsible for notifying IT of such changes in 
advance of the effective date. 
 
7.4 Incident Reporting 
Personnel who observe or suspect a policy violation, security incident, or harmful event must 
report it to their manager and the IT Department as soon as possible. Incident reports shall be 
handled in accordance with the WCG Incident Management Policy (INC-001). Personnel must 
cooperate with all investigations. 
ITEM 21
21 - 12

<!-- page 192 -->
8. Compliance & Enforcement 
 
8.1 Compliance Monitoring 
Compliance with this policy is monitored through the following mechanisms: 
• 
Automated logging and monitoring of WCG Information Resource usage by the IT 
Department 
• 
Annual security awareness training completion reporting 
• 
Periodic access reviews conducted by IT and Department Heads to verify that access 
rights remain appropriate 
• 
Review of reported incidents and suspected violations as they occur 
• 
Periodic audits of hardware, software, and cloud application registers 
 
8.2 Exceptions & Waivers 
Waivers from certain policy provisions may be requested following the WCG Waiver Process. 
Waiver requests must be submitted in writing to the Information Security Committee and must 
include a description of the provision from which a waiver is sought, the business justification, 
expected duration, and any compensating controls. Approved waivers are time-limited and 
subject to renewal. 
 
8.3 Non-Compliance 
Personnel found to have violated this policy may be subject to disciplinary action, up to and 
including termination of employment, and related civil or criminal penalties. 
 
Vendors, consultants, or contractors found to have violated this policy may be subject to 
sanctions including removal of access rights, termination of contract, and related civil or criminal 
penalties. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
ITEM 21
21 - 13

<!-- page 193 -->
Worcester County Government 
 
Generative AI Use 
Policy & Procedure 
 
ITEM 21
21 - 14

<!-- page 194 -->
1. Purpose 
 
This policy establishes clear guidelines for the ethical, secure, and responsible use of 
generative AI technologies within Worcester County Government (WCG). The policy aims to 
enable innovation while safeguarding data integrity, privacy, and transparency in alignment with 
WCG's standards and values. It defines the requirements, responsibilities, and expectations for 
the use of Generative AI in all its forms within the WCG's environment. 
 
2. Scope 
 
This policy applies to: 
• 
All employees, contractors, consultants, and third-party personnel 
• 
All systems, networks, and data owned or managed by Worcester County Government 
This policy will be revised and amended as necessary. 
 
Exclusions 
None 
 
3. Definitions 
 
Term
Definition
Artificial Intelligence (AI)
The ability of a digital computer or computer-controlled robot to 
perform tasks commonly associated with intelligent beings. AI 
systems can perform tasks such as learning, reasoning, planning, 
perception, and problem-solving.
AI Committee
A group of organizational representatives tasked with evaluating 
and advising the rest of WCG on the effectiveness and safety of AI 
technology use. The committee is composed of one delegate from 
each of the following: the County Administrator's Office, the County 
Attorney's Office, the Department of Human Resources, and the 
Department of Information Technology, including a technical lead 
from the Department of Information Technology.
Authorized Users
WCG employees, personnel under WCG contracts, volunteers, and 
all other approved individuals using information technology 
resources allocated or under the administration of WCG.

21 - 15

<!-- page 195 -->
Term
Definition
Authorized Supervisory 
Personnel
Any personnel to whom Authorized Users directly report.
Confidential Data
Data that must not be disclosed to unauthorized individuals due to 
legal, contractual, or business obligations, where disclosure may 
result in financial or reputational damage to WCG.
Restricted Data
Data that requires the highest level of protection due to legal 
penalties if mishandled.
Generative AI (GenAI)
Refers to a class of artificial intelligence-driven computer 
technology that generates new content, including text, images, 
audio, and video, in response to user prompts.
Human in the Loop 
(HITL) 
A GenAI reviewal process model which requires a human being to 
aid the GenAI system in creating the most accurate and valuable 
outputs possible.
Internal Data
Information created or bestowed upon WCG by partners or clients 
on mutual trust which shall be kept within WCG to protect business 
interests and continued client trust. If disclosed to the public, it can 
cause harm to citizens' and partners' business interests.
Large Language Model 
(LLM) 
A type of AI trained on vast amounts of text to generate natural-
sounding language. For example, chatbots or virtual assistants that 
can answer questions, perform in depth internet searches, provide 
suggestions, among many other tasks.
Information Technology 
Resources 
All computerized communications hardware, software and systems 
are owned, leased, licensed to, or accessible through WCG and 
made available for official or limited personal use. These items 
include, but are not limited to, computers, phones and other devices 
capable of transmitting and receiving data, voice communications, 
visual images, e-mail, text messages and other forms of electronic 
or digital communications, as well as the computer programs which 
support such capabilities and such systems.
Public Data
Data that is freely available and accessible to the public, without the 
need for tracking or monitoring. However, authorized users shall still 
adhere to legal requirements and organizational policies in 
handling, using, and distributing this data.
 
4. Policy Statement 
 
WCG acknowledges employees utilize GenAI, which brings risks associated with it including the 
potential for inadvertent exportation of Confidential and Restricted Data, exposure of Personal 
Data/PII, and the publication of inaccurate or misleading information. This policy establishes 
clear guidelines to prevent unauthorized Sensitive data sharing and to ensure the accuracy and 
reliability of information disseminated to the public. 
ITEM 21
21 - 16

<!-- page 196 -->
All GenAI technologies and applications used shall comply with relevant local and federal laws 
and regulations. 
 
5. Policy Requirements 
 
5.1 Approved Tools & Procurement 
• 
Only Generative AI tools that have been reviewed and approved by IT may be used for 
WCG business 
• 
All AI tools must undergo a data privacy impact assessment before deployment 
• 
Employees may not sign up for, purchase or expense AI tools with the intent to utilize 
within the WCG environment without prior authorization from IT Management. 
• 
Cloud-based AI tools must meet the county's existing data residency and vendor security 
requirements 
 
5.2 Data Classification & Handling 
• 
Confidential, sensitive, or personally identifiable information (PII) may not be entered into 
any AI tool unless it has been explicitly approved for that data classification 
• 
Protected information categories — including HIPAA-covered health data, CJIS criminal 
justice information, and juvenile records — are prohibited from AI input under any 
circumstances 
• 
Employees must assume that all data entered within any AI tools may be used to train 
the underlying model unless the vendor contractually prohibits this 
 
5.3 Acceptable Use 
• 
AI-generated content must be reviewed and verified by a HITL before use in any official 
county communication, legal document, or public-facing material 
• 
Employees may not use AI tools to make or automate decisions that materially affect 
residents (benefits eligibility, code enforcement, permitting etc.) without human review 
and approval 
• 
AI tools may not be used to generate content that impersonates county officials or 
creates misleading representations of county positions 
• 
Regardless of whether AI is used to accomplish any task, the Authorized User remains 
accountable for the final work product. Reliance on AI is not an acceptable basis for 
incomplete, inaccurate, inappropriate, or other defective work products or outcomes 
 
5.4 Public Records & Retention 
• 
AI-generated content used in official county business constitutes a public record and 
must be retained in accordance with the county's records retention schedule 
ITEM 21
21 - 17

<!-- page 197 -->
• 
Prompts submitted to AI tools for official work purposes may themselves be subject to 
public records requests and should be treated accordingly 
• 
Employees must be able to produce the source or basis for any AI-assisted work product 
upon request 
 
5.5 Accountability & Oversight 
• 
Each department deploying AI tools must designate a responsible point of contact 
accountable for appropriate use within that department 
• 
Incidents involving AI misuse, data exposure, or harmful outputs must be reported to IT 
Security within 24 hours 
• 
All AI tool usage must be logged and available for audit by IT, and the County 
Administrator's office 
 
5.6 Prohibited Uses 
• 
Entering Social Security numbers, driver's license numbers, dates of birth, or other 
Personal Identified Information PII into any AI tool not explicitly approved for that data 
type 
• 
Inputting protected health information (PHI) covered under HIPAA into any AI tool 
• 
Entering Criminal Justice Information Systems (CJIS) data, including arrest records, 
case information, or law enforcement sensitive data 
• 
Inputting juvenile records, child welfare case information, or any data protected under 
state confidentiality statutes 
• 
Using AI tools to aggregate or correlate resident data in ways that would not otherwise 
be permitted under county data governance policy 
• 
Using AI-generated outputs as the sole basis for decisions affecting resident rights, 
benefits, services, or obligations 
• 
Automating enforcement actions, citations, fines, or penalties without documented 
human review and approval 
• 
Using AI tools to screen, rank, or make recommendations in hiring, promotion, or 
disciplinary decisions without HR and Legal oversight 
• 
Allowing AI to make or recommend decisions in legal proceedings, permitting, or 
licensing without qualified professional review 
• 
Using AI tools to attempt to identify, exploit, or probe vulnerabilities in county systems or 
third-party systems 
• 
Using AI-generated code in production county systems without security review and 
approval from IT 
• 
Circumventing county security controls, content filters, or monitoring through AI tool 
usage 
 
6. Roles & Responsibilities 
 
The following roles are responsible for implementing and maintaining this policy:

7. Implementation & Procedures 
 
ITEM 21
21 - 19

<!-- page 199 -->
7.1 AI Tool Request & Approval Process 
Authorized Supervisory Personnel or Department Head reviews a GenAI request, confirms the 
use case is consistent with this policy, and forwards an approved request to the Department of 
Information Technology. The IT Department conducts a thorough review and determines if it is 
approved. 
 
7.2 Training & Awareness 
All Authorized Users must complete mandatory GenAI awareness training before being granted 
access to any approved GenAI tool. This training shall be determined by the IT Department and 
enforced by the Department Director for the Authorized User. 
 
Training must be completed annually or whenever a significant update to this policy is issued. 
The Department of Information Technology, in coordination with Human Resources, is 
responsible for administering and tracking training completion. 
 
7.3 Ongoing Monitoring & Auditing 
The Department of Information Technology shall maintain logging of GenAI tool usage across 
WCG systems. The IT Department shall conduct periodic audits — no less than annually — to 
verify efficacy.  
 
8. Compliance & Enforcement 
 
8.1 Compliance Monitoring 
Compliance with this policy is the shared responsibility of all Authorized Users, Authorized 
Supervisory Personnel, and the Department of Information Technology.  
 
8.2 Exceptions 
Exceptions require approval from both the IT Department and the County Administrator. 
Approved exceptions shall be documented, time-limited to no more than twelve (12) months, 
and subject to renewal. No exception may be granted that would result in a violation of federal 
or state law, including but not limited to HIPAA, CJIS requirements, or applicable Maryland 
statutes. 
 
ITEM 21
21 - 20

<!-- page 200 -->
8.3 Non-Compliance 
Violations of this policy may result in disciplinary action commensurate with the nature and 
severity of the violation. Disciplinary action shall be determined by the Department Director in 
coordination with the Director of Human Resources and the County Attorney. 
 
Violations involving unauthorized disclosure of HIPAA-protected, CJIS-governed, or other 
legally protected data may additionally result in referral to appropriate state or federal authorities 
and civil or criminal penalties as provided by applicable law. 
 
Contractors and third-party personnel found in violation of this policy may be removed from 
WCG premises and subject to contractual remedies as provided in their agreement with WCG.

Worcester County Government 
 
Information Security 
Policy & Procedure 
 
 
ITEM 21
21 - 22

<!-- page 202 -->
1. Purpose 
 
The purpose of the Worcester County Government Information Security Policy is to describe the 
actions and behaviors required to ensure that due care is taken to avoid inappropriate risks to 
Worcester County Government (WCG), its business partners, and its stakeholders. 
 
This policy establishes the overarching framework from which all WCG information security 
policies, standards, procedures, and guidelines are derived. It defines WCG's commitment to 
protecting the Confidentiality, Integrity, and Availability of all Information Resources maintained 
by or on behalf of WCG, and sets out the governance structure, roles, and responsibilities that 
underpin the WCG Information Security Program. 
 
Worcester County Government recognizes that its information assets are critical to the delivery 
of services to residents and to maintaining the trust of employees, partners, and the public. The 
ability to manage, control, and protect these assets has a direct and significant impact on 
WCG's operational effectiveness and its obligations under applicable law. 
 
2. Scope 
 
This policy applies to: 
• 
Any individual, entity, or process that interacts with any WCG Information Resource 
• 
All employees, contractors, consultants, volunteers, and third-party personnel who use 
or have access to WCG Information Resources 
• 
All WCG-owned, leased, or managed Information Resources, regardless of where they 
are hosted or how they are accessed 
• 
All supporting policies, standards, procedures, and guidelines that form part of the WCG 
Information Security Program 
 
Exclusions 
There are no exclusions from this policy. As the master information security policy for Worcester 
County Government, its principles apply to all WCG departments, functions, and personnel 
without exception. 
 
3. Definitions 
 
ITEM 21
21 - 23

<!-- page 203 -->
The following core terms are used throughout this policy and the WCG Information Security 
Program. For a complete list of definitions applicable across all WCG information security 
policies, see Appendix A. 
 
Term
Definition

Ensuring that Information Resources — including information, 
systems, facilities, networks, and computers — are accessible and 
usable when needed by an authorized entity.

are authorized to have access, enforced through the principle of

Confidential Information
Data that must not be disclosed to unauthorized individuals due to 
legal, contractual, or business obligations, where unauthorized 
disclosure may result in financial, operational, or reputational harm 
to WCG.
Information Resource
All computerized communications hardware, software, and systems 
owned, leased, licensed to, or accessible through WCG, made 
available for official use, including all data stored within or 
transmitted by such systems.
Information Security
The practice of protecting Information Resources against 
unauthorized access, use, disclosure, disruption, modification, or 
destruction, in order to maintain their Confidentiality, Integrity, and 
Availability.
Information Security

The body established by WCG, in accordance with the Information

and overseeing the implementation of WCG's information security

access management, encryption, or incident response.
 
4. Policy Statement 
 
Information security is a holistic discipline whose application — or lack thereof — affects all 
facets of WCG's operations and its obligations to the residents it serves. WCG is committed to 
implementing and maintaining an Information Security Program that protects the Confidentiality, 
Integrity, and Availability of all Information Resources collected or maintained by or on behalf of 
WCG, while providing value to the way county government conducts business. 
 
The three core principles of the WCG Information Security Program are: 
 
Confidentiality — Ensuring that information is accessible only to those entities that are 
authorized to have access, enforced through the need-to-know principle. 
Integrity — Protecting the accuracy and completeness of information and the methods used to 
process and manage it. 
Availability — Ensuring that Information Resources are accessible and usable when needed by 
authorized entities. 
 
WCG maintains and communicates an Information Security Program consisting of topic-specific 
policies, standards, procedures, and guidelines that collectively serve to protect the 
Confidentiality, Integrity, and Availability of WCG Information Resources using administrative, 
physical, and technical controls; provide value to WCG's operational and institutional objectives; 
and comply with all applicable regulatory, legal, and contractual requirements, including the 
HIPAA Security Rule, state breach notification laws, the PCI Data Security Standard, ISO 
27002, the NIST Cybersecurity Framework, and all other applicable federal and state laws and 
regulations. 
 
The Information Security Program is reviewed no less than annually or upon significant changes 
to the information security environment. 
 
5. Policy Requirements 
 
ITEM 21
21 - 25

<!-- page 205 -->
5.1 Information Security Program 
• 
WCG must maintain a documented Information Security Program that encompasses the 
policies, standards, procedures, guidelines, and controls necessary to protect the 
Confidentiality, Integrity, and Availability of all WCG Information Resources. 
• 
The Information Security Program must be risk-based, proportionate to the criticality of 
WCG's Information Resources, and aligned with WCG's strategic and operational 
objectives. 
• 
The Information Security Program must comply with all applicable federal and state 
laws, regulations, and contractual obligations, including but not limited to the HIPAA 
Security Rule, state breach notification laws, CJIS Security Policy, the PCI Data Security 
Standard, and applicable provisions of Maryland and Federal law. 
• 
The Information Security Program must be reviewed and updated no less than annually, 
or whenever significant changes occur to WCG's environment, technology, threat 
landscape, or applicable regulatory requirements. 
• 
Topic-specific policies, standards, procedures, and guidelines supporting this policy 
must be developed, maintained, approved, and communicated in accordance with the 
governance structure established by this policy and the Information Security Committee 
Charter. 
 
5.2 Risk Management 
• 
WCG must maintain a process for identifying, assessing, and managing risks to the 
Confidentiality, Integrity, and Availability of WCG Information Resources. 
• 
Risk assessments must be conducted on a periodic basis and whenever significant 
changes occur to WCG's information environment, technology, or threat landscape. 
• 
The results of risk assessments must be documented and used to inform the 
prioritization of information security controls, investments, and remediation activities. 
• 
A process for evaluating risks related to vendors and managing vendor relationships 
must be developed and maintained by the Information Security Officer. 
• 
Identified risks that cannot be immediately remediated must be tracked, assigned an 
owner, and subject to a documented remediation or risk acceptance plan. 
 
5.3 Security Awareness & Training 
• 
All WCG personnel — including employees, contractors, and other third-party personnel 
— must receive information security awareness training appropriate to their role and 
responsibilities. 
• 
Security awareness training must be completed before access to WCG Information 
Resources is granted to new personnel and refreshed on at least an annual basis for all 
personnel. 
• 
Personnel with significant information security responsibilities must receive role-specific 
training commensurate with those responsibilities. 
• 
The Information Security Officer is responsible for ensuring that training programs are 
current, effective, and reflective of the latest applicable legal, regulatory, and threat 
environment. 
 
ITEM 21
21 - 26

<!-- page 206 -->
5.4 Policy Compliance 
• 
All personnel must formally acknowledge their understanding of an agreement to abide 
by all applicable WCG information security policies, standards, and guidelines before 
being granted access to WCG Information Resources. 
• 
All personnel are responsible for understanding their obligations under the WCG 
Information Security Program and for seeking guidance from the Information Security 
Team when uncertain. 
• 
Compliance with this policy and all supporting topic-specific policies is mandatory. Non-
compliance will be handled in accordance with section 8.3 of this policy. 
• 
The Information Security Committee is responsible for reviewing and managing the 
policy waiver process, identifying non-compliance, and recommending appropriate 
handling. 
 
5.5 Program Evaluation & Remediation 
• 
WCG must maintain a process for planning, implementing, evaluating, and documenting 
remedial actions to address deficiencies identified in information security policies, 
procedures, and practices. 
• 
Procedures for testing and evaluating the effectiveness of the WCG Information Security 
Program must be developed and implemented by the Information Security Officer in 
accordance with stated program objectives. 
• 
The Information Security Officer, in coordination with the Information Security 
Committee, must report annually to Executive Management on the effectiveness of the 
Information Security Program, including the status of any remedial actions in progress.

7. Implementation & Procedures 
 
7.1 Information Security Program Structure 
The WCG Information Security Program is built around this policy and a suite of topic-specific 
subordinate policies, each addressing a specific control domain. All subordinate policies must 
be consistent with this policy and with each other. In the event of a conflict between this policy 
and a subordinate policy, this policy takes precedence. The current suite of WCG information 
security policies is listed in Section 9 of this document. 
 
7.2 Policy Development & Approval 
New topic-specific policies and material revisions to existing policies must be drafted by or 
under the direction of the Information Security Officer, reviewed by the Information Security 
Committee, and approved by Executive Management before taking effect. Policies must be 
communicated to all affected personnel before or upon their effective date. Minor editorial 
corrections that do not change the meaning or requirements of a policy may be approved by the 
Information Security Officer without full Committee review. 
 
7.3 Risk Assessment Process 
The Information Security Officer is responsible for maintaining a documented risk assessment 
process. Risk assessments must be conducted at least annually and following significant 
changes to WCG's environment, technology, or threat landscape. The methodology must 
include identification of Information Resources and their classification, identification of threats 
and vulnerabilities, assessment of likelihood and impact, and determination of appropriate risk 
treatment. Results must be documented and reported to the Information Security Committee. 
 
7.4 Annual Program Review & Reporting 
The Information Security Officer, in coordination with the Information Security Committee, must 
prepare an annual report to Executive Management on the effectiveness of the WCG 
Information Security Program. The report must include: a summary of the current state of the 
program; key metrics on security incidents, audit findings, and training completion; the status of 
open remediation actions; significant changes to the threat and regulatory environment; and 
recommendations for the coming year. The report must be presented to Executive Management 
no later than the first quarter of each calendar year. 
 
7.5 Vendor Risk Management 
The Information Security Officer must maintain a process for evaluating the information security 
risks associated with WCG's use of vendors and third-party service providers. Vendors with 
access to WCG Information Resources or confidential information must be assessed prior to 
ITEM 21
21 - 30

<!-- page 210 -->
engagement and on a periodic basis thereafter. Assessment criteria must include the vendor's 
security controls, data handling practices, incident response capabilities, and compliance with 
applicable regulations. Vendor contracts must include appropriate information security 
obligations. 
8. Compliance & Enforcement 
 
8.1 Compliance Monitoring 
Compliance with this policy and the WCG Information Security Program is monitored through 
the following mechanisms: 
• 
Annual review of the Information Security Program by the Information Security Officer 
and Information Security Committee 
• 
Annual information security audit conducted in accordance with the WCG Auditing Policy 
(AUD-001) 
• 
Periodic risk assessments identifying gaps in controls or compliance 
• 
Review of security incidents and near-misses for policy compliance implications 
• 
Tracking security awareness training completion rates across all departments 
• 
Annual report to Executive Management on program effectiveness and compliance 
status 
 
8.2 Exceptions & Waivers 
Waivers from certain policy provisions may be requested following the WCG Waiver Process. 
Waiver requests must be submitted in writing to the Information Security Committee and must 
include a description of the provision from which a waiver is sought, the business justification, 
expected duration, and compensating controls. The Information Security Committee reviews 
and approves or denies all waivers. Approved waivers are time-limited and subject to renewal. 
No waiver may be granted that would result in a material violation of applicable law or 
regulation. 
8.3 Non-Compliance & Sanctions 
Personnel found to have violated this policy or any subordinate WCG information security policy 
may be subject to disciplinary action, up to and including termination of employment, and 
related civil or criminal penalties. 
 
Vendors, consultants, or contractors found to have violated this policy may be subject to 
sanctions including removal of access rights, termination of contract, and related civil or criminal 
penalties. 
The Information Security Committee is responsible for identifying non-compliance and 
recommending appropriate handling to management, in coordination with the Director of Human 
Resources and the County Attorney.

21 - 31

Worcester County Government 
 
System Development 
& Procurement 
Policy & Procedure 
 
ITEM 21
21 - 32

<!-- page 212 -->
1. Purpose 
 
The purpose of the Worcester County Government System Development and Procurement 
Policy is to establish the rules for evaluating, developing, and deploying WCG Information 
Resources. This policy ensures that security requirements are incorporated from the earliest 
stages of any system acquisition or development activity, that systems are properly tested and 
accepted before production deployment, and that WCG's technology environment remains 
secure, supportable, and aligned with operational needs throughout the system lifecycle. 
 
2. Scope 
 
This policy applies to: 
• 
All individuals who participate in the procurement, development, testing, or operation of 
any WCG Information Resource 
• 
All employees, contractors, consultants, and third-party personnel involved in software 
development, system acquisition, or system deployment on behalf of WCG 
• 
All WCG Information Resources — including hardware, software, applications, and cloud 
services — acquired, developed, or deployed for use in the WCG environment 
• 
All outsourced or third-party software development undertaken on behalf of WCG 
 
Exclusions 
Minor patches and configuration updates to existing approved systems, where no new 
functionality is introduced and the change is processed through the WCG Change Control 
Policy (CCT-001), are excluded from the full system acceptance requirements of this policy. 
Such changes remain subject to CCT-001 and must not introduce new vulnerabilities or alter the 
system's security posture without a corresponding security review. 
 
3. Definitions 
 
The following terms are used throughout this policy. For a complete list of definitions, see 
Appendix A. 
 
 
ITEM 21
21 - 33

<!-- page 213 -->
Term
Definition
Acceptance Criteria
The documented operational, functional, performance, capacity, 
data classification, and security requirements that a system or 
application must satisfy before it may be deployed to the production 
environment.
Application Lifecycle
The standardized set of stages through which a WCG application 
passes from initial planning and acquisition through active use, 
maintenance, and eventual decommissioning.
Confidential Information
Data that must not be disclosed to unauthorized individuals due to 
legal, contractual, or business obligations, where unauthorized 
disclosure may result in financial or reputational harm to WCG.
Data Sanitization
The process of removing, anonymizing, or masking sensitive or 
production data from a dataset before it is used in a non-production 
environment, to prevent unauthorized exposure of confidential or 
personal information.
Development 
Environment 
A computing environment used exclusively for writing, modifying, 
and building software code, separated from testing and production 
environments.
Information Resource
All computerized communications hardware, software, and systems 
owned, leased, licensed to, or accessible through WCG, used to 
create, collect, store, or process information.
Least Privilege
The principle that access to source code, systems, and data is 
granted only to the minimum level required for a person to perform 
their authorized tasks.
Outsourced Software 
Development 
Software development performed by an external vendor, contractor, 
or third party on behalf of WCG, subject to WCG security and 
quality standards.
Production Environment
The live, operational environment in which WCG Information 
Resources are actively used to conduct county business and deliver 
services to residents.
Resource / Application 
Owner 
The individual is accountable for a specific application or 
information resource, responsible for defining acceptance criteria 
and approving deployment to production.
Secure Software 
Development Lifecycle 
(SSDLC) Standard 
The WCG document specifying security practices and controls that 
must be applied at each stage of the software development 
lifecycle, from requirements through design, coding, testing, and 
deployment.
Separation of 
Environments 
The practice of maintaining distinct, isolated computing 
environments for development, testing, and production activities to 
prevent unauthorized or accidental modification of production 
systems.

security requirements for a new system or significant enhancement 
at the onset of the acquisition or design planning phase.

Term
Definition
