Vulnerability
A weakness in a system, application, or process that could be 
exploited to compromise the security, availability, or integrity of 
WCG Information Resources.
 
4. Policy Statement 
 
Worcester County Government is committed to ensuring that all Information Resources are 
acquired, developed, and deployed in a secure, controlled, and accountable manner. Security 
must not be an afterthought — it must be embedded into the earliest stages of any procurement 
or development activity and maintained throughout the system lifecycle. Systems that are no 
longer actively supported pose an unacceptable risk and must be evaluated for replacement. 
 
All procurement, development, testing, and deployment activities must comply with this policy, 
the WCG Change Control Policy (CCT-001), the Secure Software Development Lifecycle 
(SSDLC) Standard, and all other applicable WCG policies and standards. No system or 
application may be deployed to the WCG production environment without having met all 
documented acceptance criteria. 
 
5. Policy Requirements 
 
5.1 General System Lifecycle Requirements 
• 
All applications created or deployed within the WCG IT environment must follow a 
standardized application lifecycle established by IT Management. 
• 
Applications must be actively maintained. Periodic updates must be applied to address 
known vulnerabilities in accordance with WCG vulnerability management requirements. 
• 
If an application is no longer actively supported by the vendor, developer, or responsible 
party, it must be evaluated for replacement. Continued use of an unsupported 
application requires a documented risk acceptance approved by IT Management and the 
Information Security Committee. 
• 
At the onset of any acquisition or design planning phase, security requirements must be 
identified and documented using the WCG System Security Requirements Form before 
significant design or procurement decisions are made. 
• 
All software developed within or for the WCG environment must be based on the WCG 
Secure Software Development Lifecycle (SSDLC) Standard. 
ITEM 21
21 - 35

<!-- page 215 -->
• 
Development, testing, and operational (production) environments must be separated and 
maintained as distinct, isolated environments. 
• 
Separation of duties and appropriate access controls must exist between personnel 
assigned to development and test environments and those assigned to the production 
environment. 
• 
All changes to systems must be made in accordance with the WCG Change Control 
Policy (CCT-001). 
• 
Production data must be sanitized before use in development or test environments. 
Production and test access controls must comply with production environment 
standards. 
• 
All test data and test accounts must be removed before a system transition to 
production. 
 
5.2 Secure Software Development 
• 
All software development personnel must receive training in writing secure code 
appropriate to their specific development environment before being assigned to 
development activities. Training must be refreshed annually or when a significant 
change in the development environment occurs. 
• 
IT Management must develop, implement, and maintain a Secure Software 
Development Lifecycle (SSDLC) Standard that defines security practices to be applied at 
each stage of the development process. 
• 
Access to program source code must be restricted based on the principle of least 
privilege. Source code repositories must employ access controls and audit logging. 
• 
For applications that store or transmit confidential information, security controls must be 
implemented to limit output to the minimum necessary as defined by the resource owner 
and applicable data classification requirements. 
• 
Any outsourced software development must comply with the WCG SSDLC Standard. 
Outsourcing agreements must include provisions requiring compliance with WCG 
security requirements and permitting WCG to audit or review the development process. 
• 
Modifications to externally developed or vendor-supplied software packages must be 
limited to necessary changes only, and all such changes must be strictly controlled 
through the change control process (CCT-001). 
• 
All newly developed software and all updates or revisions to existing software must be 
fully tested and accepted in accordance with documented acceptance criteria prior to 
deployment to the production environment. 
 
5.3 System Procurement 
• 
Procurement of new hardware and software must be authorized by IT Management and 
requested through WCG's established procurement process. 
• 
IT Management must perform a security review of all new hardware or software prior to 
any final purchase commitment to confirm that necessary security controls can be 
configured and that the product does not introduce unacceptable risk to the WCG 
environment. 
• 
All newly procured hardware and software must be fully tested and accepted in 
accordance with documented acceptance criteria before deployment to the production 
environment. 
ITEM 21
21 - 36

<!-- page 216 -->
• 
Deployment of new hardware and software to the production environment must be 
performed in accordance with the WCG Change Control Policy (CCT-001). 
• 
Cloud-based services and software-as-a-service (SaaS) solutions must be evaluated 
against WCG data classification, privacy, and security requirements before procurement. 
Vendor contracts must address data ownership, security controls, breach notification, 
and data return or destruction upon contract termination. 
 
5.4 System Acceptance 
• 
Acceptance criteria must be provided by the resource or application owner before any 
system or application is submitted for acceptance testing. 
Acceptance criteria must specify, at a minimum: 
◦ 
Operational and functional requirements of the application 
◦ 
Performance and capacity requirements 
◦ 
Data classification of information the system will process or store 
◦ 
Security control requirements commensurate with the data classification 
◦ 
Hardware specifications, where applicable 
◦ 
Confirmation that tests data and test accounts have been removed 
• 
All documented acceptance criteria must be satisfied — and confirmed in writing by the 
resource owner — before any system or application may be deployed to the production 
environment. 
• 
The results of acceptance testing, including any deficiencies identified and their 
remediation, must be documented and retained in accordance with the WCG Data 
Retention Schedule. 
• 
Post-deployment verification must be conducted to confirm the system is operating as 
intended and that no unintended changes to the production environment have occurred. 
 
5.5 End-of-Life & Decommissioning 
• 
When a system or application reaches end-of-life — whether due to loss of vendor 
support, functional obsolescence, or planned replacement — a decommissioning plan 
must be developed by IT Management in coordination with the resource owner. 
• 
Decommissioning must include the secure removal or migration of all data in accordance 
with the WCG Information and Data Retention Policy (RET-001) and the WCG Media 
Reuse and Destruction Standard. 
• 
Access rights to decommissioned systems must be revoked in coordination with the 
WCG Identity and Access Management Policy (IAM-001). 
• 
Decommissioning activities must be processed through the WCG Change Control Policy 
(CCT-001) and documented. 
 
6. Roles & Responsibilities 
 
The following roles are responsible for implementing and maintaining this policy:

7. Implementation & Procedures 
 
7.1 Security Requirements at Inception 
At the outset of any new system acquisition or development project, the project lead or resource 
owner must complete the WCG System Security Requirements Form in coordination with IT 
Management and the Head of Cybersecurity. This form must be completed before significant 
design, development, or procurement decisions are made. The security requirements identified 
must be incorporated into the system design, procurement specification, or development plan 
and must be reflected in the acceptance criteria. 
 
7.2 SSDLC Standard Maintenance 
IT Management is responsible for maintaining the WCG Secure Software Development 
Lifecycle Standard as a living document. The SSDLC Standard must address security 
ITEM 21
21 - 39

<!-- page 219 -->
requirements and controls at each development stage — requirements, design, coding, testing, 
and deployment — and must include secure coding practices, code review requirements, and 
security testing requirements. The Standard must be reviewed and updated at least annually 
and whenever significant changes to the development environment or threat landscape 
necessitate a revision. 
 
7.3 Environment Separation & Data Sanitization 
IT Management must maintain documented procedures confirming the separation of 
development, test, and production environments. These procedures must include access control 
requirements preventing production access by development personnel and vice versa, and data 
sanitization procedures specifying how production data is anonymized or masked before use in 
non-production environments. Compliance with these procedures must be verified as part of the 
system acceptance process. 
 
7.4 Outsourced Development Oversight 
Before engaging an external party for software development, IT Management must confirm that 
the engagement agreement includes requirements for compliance with the WCG SSDLC 
Standard, security testing obligations, code review rights, data handling requirements consistent 
with the WCG Information Classification and Management Policy (ICM-001), and WCG's right to 
audit or review the development process. IT Management must maintain oversight of the 
development process throughout the engagement and must conduct our commission security 
testing of all outsourced code before acceptance. 
 
7.5 System Acceptance Process 
Before any system or application is deployed to production, the resource owner must confirm in 
writing that all acceptance criteria have been satisfied. IT Management must confirm that test 
data and test accounts have been removed, that the system has been deployed in accordance 
with CCT-001, and that post-deployment verification has been completed. Acceptance testing 
results, including any deficiencies and their resolution, must be documented and retained in 
accordance with the WCG Data Retention Schedule. 
 
7.6 Unsupported Application Management 
IT Management must maintain a register of all WCG applications, including each application's 
support status and vendor end-of-life date. When an application approaches end-of-vendor 
support, IT Management must notify the resource owner and begin evaluating replacement 
options with sufficient lead time to avoid a gap in support. Where continued use of an 
unsupported application is unavoidable, the risk must be formally accepted by the resource 
owner and the Information Security Committee, documented, and subject to compensating 
controls and a defined remediation timeline. 
 
ITEM 21
21 - 40

<!-- page 220 -->
8. Compliance & Enforcement 
 
8.1 Compliance Monitoring 
Compliance with this policy is monitored through the following mechanisms: 
• 
Review of system acquisition and development projects by IT Management to confirm 
security requirements are captured at inception via the System Security Requirements 
Form 
• 
Review of application acceptance documentation to confirm all acceptance criteria were 
satisfied before production deployment 
• 
Annual review of the Application Support Status Register to identify applications 
approaching or past end-of-vendor-support 
• 
Periodic review of development environment separation and data sanitization controls by 
IT Management 
• 
Annual audit of system development and procurement controls as part of the WCG 
information security audit program (AUD-001) 
• 
Review of outsourced development agreements to confirm WCG security requirements 
are incorporated 
 
8.2 Exceptions & Waivers 
Waivers from certain policy provisions may be requested following the WCG Waiver Process. 
Waiver requests must be submitted in writing to the Information Security Committee and must 
include a description of the provision from which a waiver is sought, the business justification, 
expected duration, and compensating controls. Approved waivers are time-limited and subject 
to renewal. No waiver may be granted that would allow deployment to production without 
complete acceptance testing or without documented security requirements for systems handling 
confidential information. 
 
8.3 Non-Compliance & Sanctions 
Personnel found to have violated this policy — including by deploying systems to production 
without meeting acceptance criteria, bypassing environment separation controls, or failing to 
sanitize production data in non-production environments — may be subject to disciplinary 
action, up to and including termination of employment, and related civil or criminal penalties. 
 
Vendors, consultants, or contractors found to have violated this policy may be subject to 
sanctions including removal of access rights, termination of contract, and related civil or criminal 
penalties.

Worcester County Government 
 
Teleworking 
Policy & Procedure 
 
ITEM 21
21 - 42

<!-- page 222 -->
1. Purpose 
 
The purpose of the Worcester County Government Teleworking Policy is to establish the rules 
and conditions under which short-term and long-term teleworking may occur, in order to 
maintain acceptable practices regarding the use and protection of WCG Information Resources. 
This policy ensures that personnel working remotely maintain the same standard of information 
security as those working on WCG premises, and that WCG equipment, data, and systems are 
protected regardless of where work is performed. 
 
2. Scope 
 
This policy applies to: 
• 
Any individual connecting remotely to WCG Information Resources 
• 
All employees, contractors, consultants, and third-party personnel who perform WCG 
work or access WCG Information Resources from a location outside of WCG-controlled 
facilities 
• 
All WCG-provided equipment used at remote or home office locations 
• 
All WCG information accessed, processed, stored, or transmitted during remote work 
 
Exclusions 
This policy does not govern access to WCG Information Resources from WCG-managed 
locations such as satellite offices or county buildings where WCG physical and network security 
controls are fully in place. Personnel working at such locations remain subject to all applicable 
WCG policies but are not required to comply with the home office and remote connectivity 
requirements specific to this policy. 
 
3. Definitions 
 
The following terms are used throughout this policy. For a complete list of definitions, see 
Appendix A. 
 
Term
Definition
Full-Disk Encryption
A security measure that encrypts all data stored on a device's hard 
drive, ensuring that data cannot be accessed if the device is lost or 
stolen.
ITEM 21
21 - 43

<!-- page 223 -->
Term
Definition
Information Owner
The individual accountable for a specific set of WCG information, 
responsible for authorizing access and approving any printing or 
disclosure of that information.
Information Resource
All computerized communications hardware, software, and systems 
owned, leased, licensed to, or accessible through WCG, used to 
create, collect, store, or process information.
Remote Work Location
Any location outside of WCG-controlled facilities from which a 
teleworker accesses WCG Information Resources, including a 
home office, hotel, co-working space, or other off-site location.
Split Tunneling
A network configuration in which a remote device simultaneously 
connects to a VPN and to another external network, potentially 
allowing traffic to bypass VPN security controls.
Teleworker
Any WCG employee, contractor, or authorized third-party personnel 
who has been approved to perform WCG work and access WCG 
Information Resources from a remote work location.
VPN (Virtual Private 
Network) 
An approved encrypted network connection used to securely 
access WCG Information Resources from outside WCG-controlled 
facilities.
WPA2
Wi-Fi Protected Access 2 — the minimum approved wireless 
security protocol for networks used to connect to WCG Information 
Resources. Older protocols such as WPA and WEP are not 
permitted.
 
4. Policy Statement 
 
Worcester County Government recognizes that teleworking can support operational continuity 
and workforce flexibility. At the same time, remote work introduces information security risks 
that must be managed through consistent, enforceable requirements. WCG's obligation to 
protect resident data, county information, and Information Resources does not diminish because 
work is performed outside of WCG facilities. 
 
No individual may work remotely or access WCG Information Resources from outside WCG-
controlled facilities without prior approval from their manager and IT. Once approved, 
teleworkers are fully responsible for complying with this policy and all applicable WCG 
information security policies. All WCG intellectual property, data, and systems accessed or 
produced during telework remain the property of Worcester County Government. 
 
ITEM 21
21 - 44

<!-- page 224 -->
5. Policy Requirements 
 
5.1 General Teleworking Requirements 
• 
Personnel must be approved by their manager and IT prior to beginning any remote 
work or accessing WCG Information Resources remotely. Under no circumstances is a 
person permitted to work remotely without prior authorization. 
• 
Personnel are responsible for complying with all WCG policies when working using 
WCG Information Resources or on WCG time. If requirements or responsibilities are 
unclear, personnel must seek guidance from the IT Department. 
• 
All inventions, intellectual property, and proprietary information — including reports, 
drawings, blueprints, software code, computer programs, data, writings, and technical 
information — developed on WCG time and/or using WCG Information Resources are 
the property of Worcester County Government. 
• 
Teleworkers are responsible for ensuring that non-employees do not access WCG data 
in any form, including printed or electronic materials, while at the remote work location. 
• 
Teleworkers must maintain a regular work schedule. All hours must be recorded in 
accordance with standard WCG policies. Overtime and time off must receive advance 
approval in accordance with WCG's standard practices. 
• 
WCG equipment and information must be protected in accordance with their 
classification as defined in the WCG Information Classification and Management Policy 
(ICM-001). Teleworkers are responsible for protecting WCG equipment and information 
from theft, damage, or loss while in transit or at the remote work location. WCG 
equipment and documents must never be left unattended in a public area. 
• 
Personnel must follow WCG's Incidental Use provisions under the Acceptable Use 
Policy (AUP-001) when using WCG devices remotely. 
• 
Personnel must allow IT to inspect or retrieve WCG-provided equipment at any time, and 
must allow WCG to inspect or retrieve any WCG information maintained at a remote 
location. 
 
5.2 Internet Connection Requirements 
• 
Personnel must not connect WCG equipment to an unsecured Wi-Fi network, or use an 
unsecured Wi-Fi network to perform WCG work. 
• 
Wi-Fi connections used with WCG equipment must be secured with WPA2 encryption at 
minimum. The use of WPA (without the "2") or WEP is not permitted. 
• 
When connecting to any Wi-Fi network outside of WCG facilities, personnel must use the 
WCG-approved VPN solution. VPN use is mandatory for all remote connections to WCG 
Information Resources. 
• 
Personnel must not connect to another wireless network and the WCG VPN 
simultaneously (split tunneling), unless explicitly approved in advance by IT 
Management. 
For long-term or home-office arrangements, the following additional requirements apply: 
◦ 
A high-speed internet connection is required. The teleworker is responsible for 
providing and funding their own internet service, which must be of sufficient 
bandwidth to perform their regular job functions effectively. 
ITEM 21
21 - 45

<!-- page 225 -->
◦ 
IT must assess whether the teleworker's home network is adequately secured. If the 
home network does not meet WCG security requirements, IT will determine whether 
a WCG-issued wireless router is needed or whether the teleworker must comply with 
the WCG Teleworking Procedures for securing wireless networks. 
◦ 
Home wireless networks used with WCG Information Resources must be secured 
with a strong password of 16 or more characters. 
◦ 
Where possible, the home network used for WCG work should be isolated from other 
personal devices and computers in the home. 
 
5.3 Equipment Requirements 
• 
Only WCG-provided computing devices — such as WCG-issued desktops and laptops 
— may be used for remote work or accessing WCG Information Resources. The use of 
personally owned computers for WCG work is not permitted. 
• 
All WCG computing devices used for teleworking must have the following WCG-provided 
or approved security controls active and up to date: 
◦ 
Active, current antivirus software 
◦ 
Active local firewall 
◦ 
Full-disk encryption 
◦ 
Automatic screen lock after a defined period of inactivity 
• 
Teleworkers are responsible for regularly rebooting their devices to allow software 
patches and updates to be installed in a timely manner. 
• 
Personally owned devices — including but not limited to USB drives, portable hard 
drives, mobile phones, tablets, and other personal gadgets — must not be connected to 
WCG equipment, including via wireless connection, without prior IT authorization. 
• 
All maintenance of WCG-provided equipment must be performed by or pre-approved by 
IT. Teleworkers must not engage third-party repair services for WCG equipment without 
IT authorization. 
 
5.4 Printing Requirements 
• 
The printing of any non-Public WCG information at a remote location must be pre-
approved by the relevant Information Owner. 
• 
The printing of any non-Public WCG information to a public printer — such as those 
found at libraries, print shops, or hotels — is prohibited. 
• 
Personnel must be pre-approved by both IT and their manager before printing WCG 
information at a remote location. Personnel approved for remote printing must have 
access to a cross-cut or micro-cut shredder. 
• 
Wireless printing must be pre-approved by IT and requires the use of strong encryption. 
Where possible, the device used to print must be directly connected to the printer. 
• 
The printing of Confidential Information at a remote location is not permitted under any 
circumstances. 
• 
All non-Public WCG documents at a remote location must be secured when not in use 
and shredded when no longer needed, in accordance with the WCG Information 
Classification and Management Policy (ICM-001). 
 
ITEM 21
21 - 46

<!-- page 226 -->
5.5 Telephone & Communications Requirements 
• 
Remote personnel must use WCG-provided phones, headsets, or approved 
communication tools for all WCG-related calls and communications. 
• 
Teleworkers must exercise discretion when discussing WCG business in shared or 
public spaces, ensuring that conversations involving Internal or Confidential information 
cannot be overheard by unauthorized individuals. 
• 
WCG communication tools and accounts must be used only for authorized WCG 
business in compliance with the WCG Acceptable Use Policy (AUP-001). 
 
5.6 Remote Workspace Requirements 
• 
Remote workspaces must be secured to protect all WCG equipment and maintain the 
confidentiality of all WCG information. Workspaces should not be located in areas where 
unauthorized people could view screens, documents, or overhead WCG conversations. 
• 
WCG equipment and documents must be secured when unattended at the remote work 
location. Equipment must not be left visible in unattended vehicles. 
• 
Personnel must allow IT to inspect or retrieve WCG-provided equipment from the remote 
work location at any time upon request. 
• 
WCG may inspect or retrieve any WCG information maintained at a teleworker's home 
or remote work location. 
• 
The use of personal video surveillance on home entrances and exits is encouraged 
where it is feasible to deter theft or unauthorized access to WCG equipment. 
 
6. Roles & Responsibilities 
 
The following roles are responsible for implementing and maintaining this policy:

7. Implementation & Procedures 
 
7.1 Telework Approval Process 
Personnel wishing to work remotely must submit a telework request to their manager. The 
manager must approve the request based on operational need and notify IT for a security 
assessment. IT will assess the proposed remote work location's connectivity, determine whether 
additional controls — such as a WCG-issued router — are needed, and provision VPN access 
and confirm device readiness before the teleworker begins remote work. No remote access may 
be activated before both manager and IT approval is confirmed in writing. 
 
7.2 Home Network Assessment 
For long-term or home office arrangements, IT must assess the security of the teleworker's 
home network before approving remote access. The assessment must confirm that WPA2 
encryption is in use, that the wireless network password meets WCG's minimum length 
requirement (16 or more characters), and that there is no configuration that would permit split 
tunneling or expose WCG traffic to other household devices. Where the home network does not 
meet requirements, IT will provide guidance, issue a WCG router, or require the teleworker to 
comply with the WCG Teleworking Procedures before access is approved. 
 
7.3 Equipment Provisioning & Return 
IT is responsible for provisioning WCG-provided computing devices for teleworkers, configured 
with all required security controls — including antivirus, local firewall, full-disk encryption, and 
automatic screen lock — before the device is issued. All equipment issued for telework must be 
recorded in the WCG Asset Inventory Register. Upon termination of the telework arrangement, 
whether permanent or temporary, all WCG equipment must be returned to IT for inspection and 
redeployment. IT must verify that no WCG data remains on any equipment before it is 
reassigned or disposed of. 
 
7.4 Periodic Review of Remote Access 
IT Management must conduct a periodic review — no less than annually — of all active remote 
access authorizations to confirm that each authorization remains appropriate for the individual's 
current role and telework arrangement. Authorizations for individuals who no longer require 
remote access must be revoked promptly. The results of each review must be documented and 
retained in accordance with the WCG Data Retention Schedule. 
 
ITEM 21
21 - 49

<!-- page 229 -->
7.5 Incident Reporting 
Teleworkers must report any security incident occurring at their remote work location — 
including loss or theft of WCG equipment, suspected unauthorized access to WCG data, or 
damage to WCG equipment — to IT and their manager as soon as possible. Incidents must be 
handled in accordance with the WCG Incident Management Policy (INC-001). Teleworkers must 
not attempt to recover or investigate a theft independently; they must contact the appropriate 
authorities and IT immediately. 
 
8. Compliance & Enforcement 
 
8.1 Compliance Monitoring 
Compliance with this policy is monitored through the following mechanisms: 
• 
Annual review of active remote access authorizations by IT Management 
• 
Periodic review of VPN usage logs and remote access session activity 
• 
IT assessment of home network security before long-term telework arrangements are 
approved and at renewal 
• 
Device inspection upon return of WCG equipment from remote locations 
• 
Review of reported security incidents involving remote work locations, equipment loss, or 
data exposure 
• 
Annual audit of teleworking controls as part of the WCG information security audit 
program (AUD-001) 
8.2 Exceptions & Waivers 
Waivers from certain policy provisions may be requested following the WCG Waiver Process. 
Waiver requests must be submitted in writing to the Information Security Committee and must 
include a description of the provision from which a waiver is sought, the business justification, 
expected duration, and compensating controls. Approved waivers are time-limited and subject 
to renewal. No waiver may be granted that would allow remote work without prior manager and 
IT approval, or that would allow the use of personally owned computers for WCG work without 
compensating security controls. 
8.3 Non-Compliance & Sanctions 
Personnel found to have violated this policy — including by working remotely without 
authorization, using personal devices for WCG work, connecting via unsecured networks, 
allowing non-employees to access WCG data, or printing Confidential Information remotely — 
may be subject to disciplinary action, up to and including termination of employment, and 
related civil or criminal penalties. 
 
Vendors, consultants, or contractors found to have violated this policy may be subject to 
sanctions including removal of access rights, termination of contract, and related civil or criminal 
penalties.

1. Purpose 
 
The purpose of the Worcester County Government Vendor Management Policy is to describe 
the actions and behaviors required to ensure that due care is taken to avoid inappropriate risks 
to Worcester County Government, its business partners, and its stakeholders from any of its 
vendors. This policy establishes a consistent framework for evaluating vendor security posture 
before engagement, governing vendor access to WCG Information Resources and confidential 
information throughout the contract lifecycle and ensuring a secure and documented process for 
vendor offboarding. 
 
2. Scope 
 
This policy applies to: 
• 
Any individual at WCG who interacts with, sets up, or manages WCG vendors 
• 
All employees, contractors, and third-party personnel involved in vendor selection, 
contracting, oversight, or offboarding on behalf of WCG 
• 
All vendors, consultants, contractors, and third-party service providers who access, 
store, process, or transmit WCG Information Resources or confidential information 
• 
All vendor agreements, contracts, and service level agreements involving WCG 
Information Resources or confidential information 
 
Exclusions 
Vendors engaged solely for the procurement of commodity goods with no access to WCG 
Information Resources, systems, or confidential information are excluded from the risk 
assessment and contract security requirements of this policy. Such vendors remain subject to 
WCG's standard procurement processes and applicable county regulations. 
 
3. Definitions 
 
The following terms are used throughout this policy. For a complete list of definitions, see 
Appendix A. 
 
Term
Definition
Business Associate 
Agreement (BAA) 
A contract required under HIPAA between WCG and a vendor that 
creates, receives, maintains, or transmits protected health 
information (PHI) on WCG's behalf, establishing the vendor's 
obligations for safeguarding that information.
ITEM 21
21 - 52

AUP-001  |  v1.0 
Worcester County Government  |  INTERNAL 
Page 52 of 59 
Term
Definition
Confidential Information
Data that must not be disclosed to unauthorized individuals due to 
legal, contractual, or business obligations, where unauthorized 
disclosure may result in financial or reputational harm to WCG.
Critical Vendor
A vendor whose services are essential to WCG operations such 
that disruption or compromise of those services would materially 
impact WCG's ability to deliver services to residents or fulfill its 
obligations.
Information Resource
All computerized communications hardware, software, and systems 
owned, leased, licensed to, or accessible through WCG, used to 
create, collect, store, or process information.
Logical Access
The ability to connect to, query, read, write, or otherwise interact 
with WCG information systems or data through electronic means, 
as distinct from physical access to WCG facilities.
Non-Disclosure 
Agreement (NDA) 
A legally binding contract between WCG and a vendor establishing 
confidentiality obligations for WCG information to which the vendor 
is given access.
PCI DSS
Payment Card Industry Data Security Standard — a set of security 
requirements for organizations that store, process, or transmit 
payment card data. Vendors involved in WCG payment card 
processing must maintain PCI DSS compliance.
SLA (Service Level 
Agreement) 
A documented agreement between WCG and a vendor specifying 
the expected level of service, performance metrics, and 
consequences for non-compliance.
Subcontractor
A third party engaged by a vendor to perform part of the services 
delivered to WCG under a contract, who may as a result have 
access to WCG Information Resources or confidential information.
Supply Chain
The network of vendors, subcontractors, and service providers 
involved in delivering goods or services to WCG, any of whom may 
handle WCG information as part of service delivery.
Vendor
Any external organization, contractor, consultant, or individual 
engaged by WCG to provide goods or services, who may as a 
result have access to WCG Information Resources, facilities, or 
confidential information.
Vendor Non-Disclosure 
Agreement / Business 
Associate Agreement 
(Vendor NDA/BAA)
The WCG standard agreement that vendors granted access to 
WCG Information Resources must sign before engagement begins, 
establishing confidentiality, data handling, and HIPAA obligations as 
applicable.
Vendor Risk Assessment
A structured evaluation of the information security controls, 
practices, and compliance posture of a vendor with access to WCG 
Information Resources or confidential information.

Acceptable Use Policy

Worcester County Government  |  INTERNAL 
Page 53 of 59 
 
4. Policy Statement 
 
Worcester County Government relies on a range of external vendors to support its operations 
and service delivery. Each vendor relationship that involves access to WCG Information 
Resources or confidential information introduces risk that must be actively managed. WCG is 
committed to ensuring that vendors are assessed before engagement, held to clear and 
contractually binding security requirements throughout the relationship, and offboarded securely 
when the engagement ends. 
 
Vendors are extensions of WCG's operational environment in respect of the information and 
systems they access. WCG expects all vendors to take the same care with WCG information as 
WCG itself is required to take, and to proactively notify WCG of any security incidents, key 
personnel changes, or subcontracting arrangements that may affect the security of WCG 
information. 
 
5. Policy Requirements 
 
5.1 Vendor Assessments 
• 
All vendors granted access to WCG Information Resources must sign the WCG Vendor 
Non-Disclosure Agreement / Business Associate Agreement (Vendor NDA/BAA) before 
any access is provisioned. 
• 
Vendors must be evaluated prior to the commencement of any service and reassessed 
on an annual basis thereafter. 
• 
A formal vendor risk assessment must be performed on all vendors with physical or 
logical access to confidential information, and on all vendors considered critical to WCG 
operations. 
• 
Risk assessments must be performed on all requested cloud service providers before 
approval to use those services is granted. 
• 
High-risk findings identified during a vendor risk assessment must be tracked and 
followed up to verify that remediation has been completed before or as a condition of 
continued engagement. 
• 
Vendors with PCI DSS compliance requirements must have their compliance status 
reviewed on an annual basis. 
• 
The results of all vendor risk assessments must be documented and retained in 
accordance with the WCG Data Retention Schedule. 
 
5.2 Vendor Contract Requirements 
All vendor agreements and contracts involving access to WCG Information Resources or 
confidential information must specify, at a minimum: 
ITEM 21
21 - 54

<!-- page 234 -->
Acceptable Use Policy 
AUP-001  |  v1.0 
Worcester County Government  |  INTERNAL 
Page 54 of 59 
◦ 
The WCG information the vendor is authorized to access, and the purpose for which 
access is granted 
◦ 
How WCG information is to be protected by the vendor, including applicable security 
controls and classification handling requirements 
◦ 
How WCG information is to be transferred between WCG and the vendor, including 
approved transfer methods and encryption requirements 
◦ 
Acceptable methods for the return, destruction, or disposal of WCG information in 
the vendor's possession at the end of the contract, including timelines and 
confirmation requirements 
◦ 
Minimum information security requirements the vendor must maintain throughout the 
contract 
◦ 
Incident response requirements, including the timeframe within which the vendor 
must notify WCG of security incidents 
◦ 
WCG's right to audit the vendor's compliance with the contract's security 
requirements 
 
• 
If a vendor subcontracts any part of the information and communications technology 
service provided to WCG, the vendor must ensure appropriate information security 
practices are maintained throughout the supply chain and must notify WCG of any 
subcontracting arrangements before they commence. 
• 
Vendors must use WCG Information Resources only for the purpose defined in the 
business agreement. Any work outside the defined parameters of the contract must be 
approved in writing by the appropriate WCG point of contact before commencing. 
• 
Any WCG information acquired by the vendor during the contract must not be used for 
the vendor's own purposes or divulged to third parties other than approved 
subcontractors operating under equivalent confidentiality obligations. 
 
5.3 Vendor Access Management 
• 
Vendors with access to WCG Information Resources must provide authentication 
mechanisms, ensuring that all vendor access is attributable to a specific individual. 
• 
Vendor access to WCG Information Resources must be provisioned through a formal 
request and approval process and must be limited to the minimum access required for 
the defined scope of work. 
• 
Vendor access must remain disabled except when in active, authorized use for the 
purposes defined in the contract. 
• 
New vendors must provide WCG with a list of key personnel who will be working on the 
contract before access is provisioned. 
• 
Vendors must notify WCG of key staff changes — including additions, departures, and 
role changes — within 24 hours of the change occurring. 
• 
Upon the departure of a vendor employee from the contract for any reason, the vendor 
must ensure that all WCG sensitive information in that individual's possession is 
collected and returned to WCG. 
• 
WCG IT will provide a technical point of contact for each vendor relationship. That point 
of contact is responsible for working with the vendor to confirm compliance with WCG's 
policies and contract requirements. 
 
ITEM 21
21 - 55

<!-- page 235 -->
Acceptable Use Policy 
AUP-001  |  v1.0 
Worcester County Government  |  INTERNAL 
Page 55 of 59 
5.4 Vendor Activity Logging 
• 
Vendors' work activities must be recorded in a log and made available to WCG IT 
Management upon request as it pertains to any WCG Resources. 
• 
Vendor activity logs must include but are not limited to personnel on-site or with active 
access, password changes, project milestones and deliverables, and arrival and 
departure times for on-site work. 
• 
Logs must be maintained in sufficient detail to support WCG's audit rights and incident 
investigation requirements. 
 
5.5 Vendor Performance Monitoring 
• 
Vendor performance must be reviewed annually to assess compliance with contractual 
requirements and SLAs. 
• 
In the event of non-compliance with contract requirements or SLAs, regular performance 
meetings must be held with the vendor until requirements are met. Persistent non-
compliance may result in contract remediation including termination. 
• 
Vendors must report all security incidents directly to the appropriate WCG IT personnel 
within the timeframe defined in the contract. Vendor incident reports must be handled in 
accordance with the WCG Incident Management Policy (INC-001). 
 
5.6 Vendor Offboarding 
• 
Upon termination of a vendor contract — whether by expiry, termination for cause, or 
mutual agreement — the vendor must be formally reminded of their ongoing 
confidentiality and non-disclosure obligations. 
• 
Upon termination or at WCG's request, the vendor must immediately surrender all WCG 
badges, access cards, equipment, and supplies. Any equipment or supplies to be 
retained by the vendor must be documented and authorized by WCG IT Management in 
writing. 
• 
All logical access granted to vendor personnel must be revoked on or before the contract 
termination date, in accordance with the WCG Identity and Access Management Policy 
(IAM-001). 
• 
The vendor must confirm in writing that all WCG information in their possession — 
including information held by approved subcontractors — has been returned or securely 
destroyed in accordance with the contract's data disposal requirements and the WCG 
Media Reuse and Destruction Standard. 
• 
WCG IT must remove or revoke any vendor-specific accounts, credentials, or access 
configurations from WCG systems upon contract termination. 
 
6. Roles & Responsibilities 
 
The following roles are responsible for implementing and maintaining this policy:

7. Implementation & Procedures
7.1 Vendor Onboarding Process 
Before any vendor is granted access to WCG Information Resources or confidential information, 
the following steps must be completed: the vendor must sign the WCG Vendor NDA/BAA; a 
vendor risk assessment must be completed and reviewed by IT Management and the Head of 
Cybersecurity; the contract must be reviewed by the County Attorney's Office to confirm 
required security provisions are included; and vendor access must be provisioned by IT 
Management only after all preceding steps are complete. A record of completed onboarding 
steps must be maintained in the WCG Vendor Register. 
7.2 Vendor Risk Assessment Process 
The Head of Cybersecurity is responsible for maintaining a documented vendor risk assessment 
methodology. Assessments must evaluate the vendor's security controls, data handling 
practices, incident response capabilities, subcontracting arrangements, and applicable 
regulatory compliance (including PCI DSS where relevant). Assessments must be conducted 
before engagement and annually thereafter. Cloud service providers must be assessed before 
approval is granted. High-risk findings must be tracked in the WCG Vendor Risk Register and 
followed up until remediation is confirmed. 
7.3 Contract Security Requirements Review 
Every vendor contract involving WCG Information Resources or confidential information must be 
reviewed by the County Attorney's Office before execution to confirm that all required security 
provisions are present. IT Management must provide input on technical requirements. The 
Information Security Committee must be consulted on contracts involving critical vendors or 
high volumes of confidential information. Contracts that lack required security provisions must 
not be executed without the approval of the County Attorney's Office and the Information 
Security Committee. 
ITEM 21
21 - 58

<!-- page 238 -->
Acceptable Use Policy 
AUP-001  |  v1.0 
Worcester County Government  |  INTERNAL 
Page 58 of 59 
7.4 Annual Vendor Performance Review 
Department Heads or designated contract managers must conduct an annual review of each 
active vendor relationship, assessing compliance with contractual requirements and SLAs. The 
review must include confirmation that the vendor's risk assessment remains current, that no 
unresolved high-risk findings are outstanding, and that the vendor's access rights remain 
appropriate for the current scope of work. Review results must be documented and retained in 
the WCG Vendor Register. 
 
7.5 Vendor Offboarding Process 
Upon contract termination, the contract manager must notify IT Management at least five 
business days in advance (or immediately in case of an emergency termination). IT 
Management must revoke all vendor logical access on or before the termination date, 
coordinate the return of all WCG equipment and access credentials, and obtain written 
confirmation from the vendor that all WCG information has been returned or destroyed. The 
County Attorney's Office must ensure the vendor is formally notified of their ongoing 
confidentiality obligations. All offboarding activities must be documented in the WCG Vendor 
Register. 
 
8. Compliance & Enforcement 
 
8.1 Compliance Monitoring 
Compliance with this policy is monitored through the following mechanisms: 
• 
Annual vendor risk assessments confirming the security posture of all vendors with 
access to WCG Information Resources or confidential information 
• 
Annual vendor performance reviews assessing compliance with contractual 
requirements and SLAs 
• 
Annual review of PCI DSS compliance status for applicable vendors 
• 
Periodic review of the WCG Vendor Register to confirm all active vendor agreements are 
current and access rights remain appropriate 
• 
Tracking of high-risk assessment findings in the WCG Vendor Risk Register until 
remediation is confirmed 
• 
Review of vendor-reported security incidents and WCG's response in accordance with 
INC-001 
• 
Annual audit of vendor management controls as part of the WCG information security 
audit program (AUD-001) 
 
8.2 Exceptions & Waivers 
Waivers from certain policy provisions may be requested following the WCG Waiver Process. 
Waiver requests must be submitted in writing to the Information Security Committee and must 
include a description of the provision from which a waiver is sought, the business justification, 
expected duration, and compensating controls. Waivers involving contract security requirements 
ITEM 21
21 - 59

<!-- page 239 -->
Acceptable Use Policy 
AUP-001  |  v1.0 
Worcester County Government  |  INTERNAL 
Page 59 of 59
must be reviewed by the County Attorney's Office before approval. No waiver may be granted 
that would allow a vendor to access WCG Information Resources without signing the Vendor 
NDA/BAA, or that would allow vendor access to confidential information without a completed 
risk assessment. 
8.3 Non-Compliance & Sanctions 
WCG personnel found to have violated this policy — including by engaging vendors without 
completing required risk assessments, executing contracts without required security provisions, 
or failing to revoke vendor access upon contract termination — may be subject to disciplinary 
action, up to and including termination of employment, and related civil or criminal penalties. 
Vendors found to have violated this policy or their contractual security obligations may be 
subject to contract remedies including suspension of access, financial penalties as provided for 
in the contract, termination of contract, and referral to appropriate authorities where legal 
violations are involved. 
9. Review & Revision History
This policy shall be reviewed annually or whenever significant changes occur to WCG vendor 
management practices, applicable regulations, or the threat environment.

Reference: 
 
Human Services Article - Annotated Code of Maryland - Section 3-501 
 
Appointed by:  
County Commissioners 
 
Functions: 
 
Advisory

the State Department of Human Resources. 
Act as liaison between Social Services Dept. and County Commissioners. 
Advocate social services programs on local, state and federal level.

9 to 13 members/3 years

aggregate give a countywide representative character.

Except County Commissioner, members may not hold public office.

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
Weston S. Young, Chief Administrative Officer 
FROM: 
Jennifer K. Keener, AICP, Director 
DATE: 
June 29, 2026 
RE: 
Work Session: Proposed Text Amendment – Data Centers and Cable Landing Stations 
************************************************************************************ 
By direction of the Worcester County Commissioners on Tuesday, June 2, 2026, I have begun drafting 
legislation to address data centers and cable landing stations in the Worcester County Zoning Code. The 
amendment will focus on five policy recommendations outlined below. At this time, I am requesting the 
Commissioners’ input on the potential zoning districts and any feedback on the recommendations listed. 
Policy Recommendation # 1 - Clearly Define the Use: 
Technology evolves faster than zoning codes. Planners across the state and the nation are examining 
data centers and trying to understand the various operational scales that currently exist. Additionally, 
many coastal communities are grappling with how to classify cable landing stations. This amendment is 
the first step in regulating data centers and cable landing stations. 
The proposed definition of a data center mirrors other Maryland jurisdictions that have established 
regulations. Limitations on the size of the buildings and the specific lot requirements can be established 
in district regulations to prevent hyperscale data centers from locating in the county. 
DATA CENTER - A facility consisting of one or more buildings used primarily for the storage, 
management, processing, and transmission of digital data, and which houses computer or 
network equipment, systems, servers, appliances, and other associated components related to 
digital data operations. The facility may also include customary accessory uses such as an office, 
air handlers, power generators and storage, water cooling and storage facilities, and associated 
utility infrastructure needed to support sustained operations of the digital infrastructure. 
CABLE LANDING STATION - A facility where subsea cable systems terminate and digital 
data is transmitted to land-based telecommunications networks. The facility may also include 
customary accessory uses such as an office, air handlers, power generators and storage, 
telecommunication carrier interconnection equipment and associated utility infrastructure needed 
to support sustained operations of the digital infrastructure. A cable landing station shall not 
include data storage, management, processing or computation. 
ITEM 23
23 - 1

<!-- page 257 -->
Policy Recommendation # 2 - Public Involvement in the Planning Process: 
As evidenced by the outpouring of written and public comment during the May 14, 2026, Town Hall 
meeting, land use planning requires careful consideration to ensure that the proposed use and facilities 
fit within their surroundings and protect areas that are most important to our county residents. By 
establishing the uses as a special exception in the appropriate zoning districts, it allows the public an 
opportunity to be a part of the planning process and comment upon the site-specific proposal during a 
public hearing before the Board of Zoning Appeals. 
Action requested: Prior to developing district specific language, I am requesting that the County 
Commissioners evaluate whether the following zoning districts should include data centers and cable 
landing stations as special exception uses: 
•
I-1 Light Industrial District: data centers and cable landing stations
•
I-2 Heavy Industrial District: data centers and cable landing stations
•
CA Commercial Airport District: cable landing stations
•
C-2 General Commercial District: cable landing stations
•
C-3 Highway Commercial District: cable landing stations
A map of the I-1 and I-2 District properties is attached. The CA District is limited to the Ocean City 
Municipal Airport, and the C-3 District properties are limited to the US Route 50/ Samuel Bowen 
Boulevard corridor. 
Policy Recommendation # 3 - Ensure Proper Design Aesthetics: 
To reduce the negative impacts of data centers and cable landing stations, Worcester County may set 
design criteria that will enhance the community rather than detract from it. Worcester County already 
has established Design Guidelines and Standards that would apply standards for building façades, 
lighting, sidewalks/bike paths and landscaping to projects subject to the major site plan review process 
(buildings of 10,000 square feet or greater). Additional protections can be proposed through enhancing 
setbacks, lot area, and landscaping requirements beyond the typical standards. 
Policy Recommendation # 4 - Noise Impacts: 
I recommend adding language requiring a sound impact assessment to be prepared by a qualified third-
party consultant for every application for a data center or cable landing station. The assessments shall 
include a listing of all equipment included in the evaluation and identify the potential full build-out of 
the project requested under site plan review. Mitigation measures shall be identified. Impacts can also be 
minimized by: 
•
Mandating that testing of generators and other external equipment be restricted to normal
daytime/ business hours.
•
Setting lower thresholds for the measurement of sound at the property line than the typical uses
permitted in the respective zoning districts under § ZS 1-346. This may be appropriate for
properties where the use would adjoin a residential use or zone, if permitted.
Policy Recommendation # 5 - Ensure Sustainable Operations: 
Staff as well as public engagement findings identified questions and concerns regarding energy grid 
reliability, potential heavy water usage, and protecting site specific assets. Jurisdictions such as Prince 
George's County and Montgomery County are considering the obligation for an operational 
ITEM 23
23 - 2

<!-- page 258 -->
sustainability plan that would require the developer to outline detailed strategies for water and energy 
consumption, grid upgrades, air quality and integration of mitigation measures into the site design. 
Additionally, some jurisdictions are requiring generators to meet or exceed Tier 4 or equivalent emission 
standards as defined by the U.S. Environmental Protection Agency (EPA) and have established 
regulations for fuel storage tanks. 
 
Next Steps: Based on the feedback received at the work session, the draft bill language will be finalized. 
As a zoning bill, the Planning Commission shall review the legislation and provide a recommendation. 
The recommendation will be forwarded to the County Commissioners for bill introduction and the 
scheduling of a public hearing. 
 
Research Links: Below you will find links to information on various reports that have been prepared on 
Data Centers. Additionally, code language and policy recommendations from other jurisdictions can be 
found attached to this memo, including Frederick County, Calvert County, Prince George’s County, 
Baltimore County, Montgomery County, and Anne Arundel County. 
 
• Final Report on Qualified Data Centers in Prince George’s County:

• American Planning Association: https://www.planning.org/planning/2021/summer/data-centers-
evolved-a-primer-for-planners/

https://knowledge.uli.org/-/media/files/research-reports/2024/uli-data-center-
whitepaper_hm_2024-11-12_final-final-round.pdf?bcs-agent-scanner=738a2c16-c52d-214d-

County’s research on the use classification. 
 
Jurisdiction: Virginia Beach, VA 
Use Classification: Fiber Optics Transmission Facility* 
Current Zoning: I-1 Industrial District, Corporate Landing Business Park 
Building Size: 20,700 square feet with potential for 8,000 square foot expansion 
Users: Telefonica, Microsoft, Facebook 
Is there a data center component? Not that staff was aware. 
Other information: Condition of approval – Approval required for schedule on generator 
routine maintenance hours. No known complaints.

Jurisdiction: Jacksonville Beach, FL 
Use Classification: essential service/ public utility* 
Current Zoning: I-1 Industrial District 
Building Size: est. 18,000 square feet – retrofit of existing building 
Users: not identified 
Is there a data center component? No. 
Other information: Immediately adjacent to residential dwellings. No known complaints. 
*Zoning Code reference is attached. 
 
 
Jurisdiction: Seagirt/ Manasquan, NJ 
Use Classification: office with no public entry 
Current Zoning: light industrial and commercial 
Building Size: not identified 
Users: not identified 
Is there a data center component? No. 
Other information: Constructed 20+ years ago. Part of NJ’s list of critical infrastructure. 
Adjacent to residential dwellings. No history of complaints.

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
Worcester County Commissioners 
FROM: 
Charlene Sharpe, Legislative Analyst 
DATE: 
July 6, 2026 
RE: 
Public Service Commission letter 
Comments regarding the Power Plant Research Program’s Decommissioning Recommendation are being accepted 
through July 10. We thought this would be a good opportunity to advocate for the code provisions we’ve put in 
place, which require a decommissioning plan and bond. As a result, we are seeking approval to submit the attached 
letter, written by DRP Director Jennifer Keener and reviewed by County Attorney Roscoe Leslie, to the Public Service 
Commission.

WORCESTER COUNTY
DEPARTMENT OF DEVELOPMENT REVIEW AND PERMITTING
GOVERNMENT CENTER ONE WEST MARKET STREET, ROOM 1201 
SNOW HILL, MARYLAND 21863 
TEL:410.632.1200 
http://www.co.worcester.md.us/departments/drp
July 2, 2026 
Andrew S. Johnston, Executive Secretary 
Maryland Public Service Commission  
William Donald Schaefer Tower 
6 St. Paul Street, 16th Floor 
Baltimore, MD 21202 
Re: 
Case No. 9873 –Decommissioning Plan Requirements 
Worcester County Comments 
Dear Mr. Johnston: 
Thank you for the opportunity to provide comments on the decommissioning plan and bond 
requirements for solar projects between 1 MW and 5 MW. Locally, Worcester County has an 
established decommissioning plan and bond requirement in the Zoning Code. I have reviewed the draft 
cost estimation tool and support its use in making the bond calculations consistent across the state. The 
proposed decommissioning activities listed in the estimation tool appear to be consistent with our local 
amendments, except for allowing significant exemptions for salvage value. It is imperative that local 
jurisdictions retain control over decommissioning bonds because our constituents bear the most 
physical, environmental and financial risk for abandoned projects. 
There is currently a limited, high volatility market for solar module recycling and concerns that the 
actual costs to recycle the modules and components could exceed the raw material scrap value. This 
creates a potential financial risk and could leave the county’s taxpayers on the hook to pay for the 
decommissioning should the bond not cover all the work.  
Therefore, using the cost estimation tool without salvage value deductions at the local level would be 
preferred over preemptive measures to handle all bonding at the state level. It is our position that the 
local jurisdictions are in a better position to manage the decommissioning activities on the property, 
coordinate with contractors and ensure that the timelines for decommissioning are enforced. 
Again, thank you for the opportunity to provide feedback. Please reach out with any questions. 
Sincerely, 
Jennifer K. Keener, AICP 
Director
